Nori motives (and mixed Hodge modules) with integral coefficients
Raphaël Ruimy, Swann Tubach

Abstract
We construct abelian categories of integral Nori motivic sheaves over a scheme of characteristic zero. The first step is to study the presentable derived category of Nori motives over a field. Next we construct an algebra in étale motives such that modules over it afford a t-structure that restricts to constructible objects. This category of integral Nori motives has the six operations and arc-descent. We finish by providing analogous constructions and results for mixed Hodge modules on schemes over the reals.

Introduction

A cohomology theory of algebraic varieties can be represented by a motivic ring spectrum, or motivic algebra, and vice versa. Finding the universal cohomology of algebraic varieties is therefore the same as finding a universal algebra in the category of étale motives. The most natural candidate is the unit object, which represents étale motivic cohomology. In characteristic zero, all known cohomology theories compare to the Betti motivic algebra, that is the algebra corresponding to the cohomology theory given by singular cohomology of the complex points. This algebra has the convenient property that modules over it afford a t-structure. In this paper, we define the Nori algebra which is universal among motivic algebras that compare to the Betti algebra and for which modules over them have a reasonable t-structure.

The above description is derived in nature: the category of étale motives is constructed out of a derived category, and we what we call a cohomology theory is a functor

\mathrm{R}\Gamma\colon\mathrm{Sm}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{A})

sending a smooth variety over a field
k
to a complex of objects (in some abelian category
\mathcal{A}
); this contains more information than all the
\mathrm{H}^{i}
of this complex. There is however another construction of a universal cohomology theory, that has a more abelian nature. This construction is due to Nori. We recall it before going into more details about our construction.

Motives over a field.

We fix for now a subfield
k
of
\mathbb{C}
. Nori’s fundamental insight is that, as every known cohomology theory of
k
-varieties can be compared to singular cohomology, one should start from the abelian groups

\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{B}}(X,Z):=\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},Z^{\mathrm{an}})

given by the singular cohomology of the analytification of any algebraic variety
X
over
k
, relative to the analytification of a closed subvariety
Z\subseteq X
. The category
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
of Nori motives over
k
is then an abelian category containing an object
\mathrm{H}^{i}(X,Z)
which, in a sense that will be made precise, is the abelian group
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{B}}(X,Z)
endowed with the finest structure that is preserved by algebraic maps; this mysterious structure is in fact the action of an affine group scheme over
\mathbb{Z}
: the motivic Galois group
\mathrm{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}(k)
.
There is a quiver (or oriented graph)
\mathrm{Pairs}_{k}
, constructed out of the morphisms between the
\mathrm{H}^{i}(X,Z)
giving functoriality in
(X,Y)
, boundaries for the long exact sequence of cohomology and expressing the fact that the Lefschetz object
\mathbb{Z}(-1):=\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k},\emptyset)
is invertible (see
[
HMS17
, Definition 9.3.2]
). For Nori, a cohomology theory is a representation

\mathrm{H}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Pairs}_{k}\to\mathcal{A}

of the quiver of pairs, so that our
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{B}}
gives a cohomology theory
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{\mathrm{B}}
. The abelian category of Nori motives is the universal representation of the quiver
\mathrm{Pairs}_{k}
that factors the Betti representation in a faithful way:
for every diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Pairs}_{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{ft}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{*}_{\mathrm{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{*}_{\mathrm{univ}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{f}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\mathrm{B}}}

of full arrows, with
\mathrm{f}_{\mathcal{A}}
faithful and exact, there exists an essentially unique faithful exact functor
R_{\mathcal{A}}
fitting in the dashed arrow. In such a diagram, one can see
\mathcal{A}
as additional structure on singular cohomology of pairs, so that it is clear that
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
provides the best possible structure.

This abelian approach is linked to the above derived approach. The
\infty
-category of geometric étale motives
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
with integral coefficients is made in such way that any enriched mixed Weil cohomology theory in the sense of Cisinski and Déglise’s
[
CD12
]

\mathrm{R}\Gamma\colon\mathrm{Sm}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{D}(\mathcal{A})
factors through the canonical functor
h\colon\mathrm{Sm}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
(which sends an smooth variety to its cohomological motive). The following result provides the link between Voevodsky and Nori motives.

Theorem A

(Nori, Harrer, Choudhury, Gallauer
[
Har16
,
CG17
]
)
.

There exists a Nori realisation functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))

such that the composition with the Betti realisation of Nori motives
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\xrightarrow{R_{\mathrm{B}}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{fr}})
gives the derived Betti cohomology theory
C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}\colon\mathrm{Sm}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{ft}})
.

This shows that any cohomology theory
à la
Nori induces a derived cohomology theory. Conversely there exists a representation
\mathrm{Pairs}_{k}\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
, therefore any derived cohomology theory induces a representation of the quiver of pairs. Giving a formal meaning to these considerations led to a new universal property of Nori motives (
[
HMS17
, Corollary 10.1.7]
):
for every diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{ft}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{f}_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\mathcal{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\mathrm{B}}}

of full arrows with
\mathrm{H}^{*}_{\mathcal{A}}
a cohomological functor and
\mathrm{f}_{\mathcal{A}}
a faithful exact functor, there exists a unique faithful exact functor
R_{\mathcal{A}}
filling the dashed arrow.

Rational Nori motivic sheaves.
This new point of view opened a way to construct relative versions of Nori motives. Indeed, why stop at abelian groups? Sheaves of abelian groups are so nice. Using the method of Nori with a diagram of pairs, Arapura (
[
Ara13
]
) and Ivorra (
[
Ivo17
]
) constructed abelian categories of Nori motivic sheaves that are the universal recipient of a cohomology theory of relative pairs of
k
-varieties modelled on constructible sheaves for Arapura, and on perverse sheaves for Ivorra. Although we will not go into details, let us say that these categories have simple universal properties but lack a critical structure that one expects when dealing with sheaves: a six functors formalism. Indeed, for a theory of sheaves
\mathcal{A}(X)
to be efficient, notably for some reductions, it is desirable to dispose of the six operations: those are adjunctions

f^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(X))\leftrightarrows\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(Y))\colon f_{*},

f_{!}\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(Y))\leftrightarrows\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(X))\colon f^{!},

for
f\colon Y\to X
a morphism of varieties, and adjunctions

-\otimes F\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(X))\leftrightarrows\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(X))\colon\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(F,-)

for
F
belonging to
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}(X))
. The abelian categories constructed by Arapura and Ivorra do not have all of the six operations, because it is very hard to exhibit some morphisms of the quivers of pairs that would induce the above operations. This is where using the new universal property of Nori motives is critical: working with rational coefficients, if instead of taking a diagram of pairs, one takes the category
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
of relative étale motives and uses the perverse Betti realisation, one can construct an abelian category
{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
of perverse motivic sheaves which has the new universal property. This is what Ivorra and S. Morel do in their paper
[
IM24
]
. As promised, using the full strength of the functoriality of
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
(that affords the six operations), this allows for the construction of the six operations:

Theorem B

(Ivorra, Morel, Terenzi
[
IM24
,
Ter26
]
)
.

Over quasi-projective
k
-varieties, the derived category
\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))
of perverse motives is endowed with the six operations.

At that point, the two sheaf theories
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})
and
\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))
did not talk to each other because of the way they were constructed: the first one is very derived, and its universal property is highly
\infty
-categorical, whereas the second one is based on abelian constructions, and had poor
\infty
-categorical properties. In
[
Tub25b
]
, the second author of the present paper proved that the constructions of Ivorra-Morel and Terenzi had canonical
\infty
-categorical lifts, so that the universal property of the functor
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})
proven by Drew and Gallauer in
[
DG22
]
would provide a realisation functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))

that commutes with the six operations. This realisation functor induces an adjunction

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))\colon\rho_{*}^{\mathrm{N}}

between the associated functors of presentable
\infty
-categories. As
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
is monoidal, its right adjoint
\rho_{*}^{\mathrm{N}}
is lax-monoidal and thus creates a commutative algebra object
\mathscr{N}_{(-),\mathbb{Q}}:=\rho_{*}^{\mathrm{N}}\mathbb{Q}_{(-)}
in the presentable
\infty
-category of étale motives
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})
.
Hence, for every variety
X
the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
factors through a refined realisation

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{-\otimes\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\rho_{\mathrm{N}}}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Q})).

Theorem C

(
[
Tub25b
]
)
.

Let
\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q}):=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{(-),\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))
. The induced functor

\widetilde{\rho_{\mathrm{N}}}\colon\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))

is an equivalence. In particular, for every
k
-variety of structural map
p_{X}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
the canonical map
p_{X}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}
is an equivalence.

Furthermore, if
K
is a field extension of
k
included in
\mathbb{C}
, the map
(\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}})|_{K}\to\mathscr{N}_{K,\mathbb{Q}}
is an equivalence.

The whole sheaf theory
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))
can therefore be recovered from the algebra
\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
.
The last part of the theorem allows to extend the functor
\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})
to any
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme
p_{X}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
by setting
\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}=p_{X}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q}}
. Hence, everything can be recovered from the algebra
\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q}}
and the functor
\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})
can be enhanced into a six functors formalism thanks to the results of
[
AGV22
]
.

Main results of this paper.

The goal of this paper is to extend the above picture to integral coefficients. We first provide a new construction of integral Nori motivic sheaves. The idea is to first define a presentable stable
\infty
-category by taking modules over some algebra (the
Nori algebra
) and then prove that this category is endowed with an ordinary t-structure and a perverse t-structure which provides abelian categories of Nori motivic sheaves. The advantage of our construction over that of
[
Ara13
]
and
[
Ivo17
]
is that our categories are endowed with a full six functors formalism which still remains open for Arapura and Ivorra’s construction. Furthermore, they are canonically endowed with a realisation functor from étale motives. The existence of the six functors implies one of the main results of
[
Ara05
]
(see
??
). We are also able to prove remarkable descent properties (namely arc-descent) on our category of Nori motives. Finally, we can compare our stable version of Nori motives with the derived categories of both of its hearts; for the ordinary heart we obtain an equivalence and we formulate a conjecture on the perverse heart which implies the equivalence between its bounded derived category and geometric Nori motives as defined below.

The Nori algebra.

The construction of Ivorra and Morel could only work with rational coefficients: their construction notably relies on the fact that the Verdier duality functor is perverse t-exact and can be expressed as a derived functor on the category of perverse sheaves using
[
Bei87
]
. This is not at all the case with integral coefficients as this was pointed out in
[
BBD82
, Section 3.3]
. They also use in a crucial manner the motivic unipotent nearby cycle functor constructed by Ayoub, and this functor is behaves badly with integral coefficients
[
Ayo07
, Remarque 3.4.14]
. However, the following idea to extend their work is very natural and is the starting point of the present paper: note first that in any
\mathbb{Z}
-linear stable presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
and any object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
, there is a Hasse fracture square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{n}A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\lim_{n}A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where the limits are taken over the poset
\mathbb{N}^{*}
of nonzero integers ordered by divisibility (so that if
A=\mathbb{Z}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})
, the top right object is just the profinite completion
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
of the integers).
If one manages to construct a map

\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}\to(\lim_{n}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, then one could use a Hasse fracture square to
define
an algebra
\mathscr{N}_{k}:=\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Z}}
in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
such that for every
k
-variety
p_{X}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
of modules over
p_{X}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{k}
in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is entitled the name of integral Nori motives over
X
.

To do so, one solution is to study integral Nori motives over a field. As explained above, we have an abelian category
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
of Nori motives over
k
. Thus there are two naive possibilities for a presentable
\infty
-category of Nori motives over
k
: one could take the unbounded derived category
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
of the indisation of Nori motives, or take the indisation
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
of the bounded derived category of Nori motives. These two categories do not give the correct presentable category as explained in
??
.

Nonetheless, we first consider the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
, as it is easier to get a grasp of. In fact as mentionned above, the work of Choudhury-Gallauer (
??
) gives us a symmetric monoidal functor

\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))).

Thus we may take a right adjoint
F
of this functor, and
define

\mathscr{N}_{k}
as the value of
F
at the unit object of
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})))
. Using rigidity for Nori motives over a field (as proven by Nori,
??
), and the fact that
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
is a full subcategory if
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})))
, we manage to prove (
??
and
??
) that the algebra
\mathscr{N}_{k}
does fit in a Hasse fracture square

As a consequence, we have:

Theorem D

(
??
and
??
)
.

Let
k
be a subfield of the complex numbers. Consider the factorisation of
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
through the modules category

\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{k}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})).

This functor is fully faithful when restricted to the thick subcategory
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
generated by the
M_{k}(X)(i)\otimes\mathscr{N}_{k}
for
X
smooth over
k
and induces an equivalence between the latter and
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
.

Moreover, if
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}(K)\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
is a morphism of fields, then the canonical comparisaon map

f^{*}\mathscr{N}_{k}\to\mathscr{N}_{K}

is an equivalence.

Integral Nori motivic sheaves.

For any qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme with a structural map
p_{X}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
, we now set
\mathscr{N}_{X}:=p_{X}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}
. If
\Lambda
is a commutative ring, we set
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))
; we have a six functors formalism on
\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
by
??
. We have therefore produced a presentable stable
\infty
-category of Nori motives. Furthermore, we have a Nori realisation functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)

which is simply the functor
-\otimes\mathscr{N}_{X}
. It is compatible with the six functors in the following sense:

Theorem E

(
??
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Over qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, the Nori realisation
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
is compatible with the operations of type
f^{*}
,
f_{*}
,
f_{!}
,
f^{!}
,
\otimes
and
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,-)
for
M
of geometric origin.

Then next steps will aim to produce a small abelian category from
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)
.
To that end, we study the subcategory
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)
made of Nori motives of geometric origin (
??
). In
[
RT25
]
, we proved a categorical criterion for being geometric which is
constructibility
as well as continuity and descent properties of the category
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
of geometric étale motives. The same properties hold for Nori motives, more precisely:

Theorem F

(
??
)
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. The functor
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
is finitary. Furthermore,

1.

If
X
is a qcqs
\Lambda
-finite (see
??
)
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, then
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)=\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)^{\omega}
.

2.

If the ring
\Lambda
is regular, a Nori motive
M
over a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension is of geometric origin if and only if it is
constructible
, that is for any open affine
U\subseteq X
, there is a finite stratification
U_{i}\subseteq U
made of constructible locally closed subschemes such that each
M|_{U_{i}}
is dualisable.

In addition, the six functors from
\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
induce a six-functors formalism on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
over quasi-excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension (
??
) and we also have a Verdier duality functor under some mild resolution of singularities assumptions (see
??
).

t-structures on geometric motives.

The next step is to define a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
in order to define abelian categories of Nori motivic sheaves. By analogy with constructible sheaves, two t-structures should exist: the ordinary t-structure and the perverse t-structure. The first we construct is the ordinary one. Assume that
X
is of finite-type over a subfield
k
of
\mathbb{C}
and recall that following Saito (
[
Sai90
, 4.6]
), the second author defined in
[
Tub25b
]
a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Q})(=\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Q})))
such that the Betti realisation functor

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})

to the category of constructible analytic complexes is t-exact when the latter is endowed with its ordinary t-structure. When
k
is algebraically closed, this can be used to define a t-structure integrally. Indeed, we prove that a geometric motive with coefficients
\mathbb{Z}
is exactly a geometric motive with coefficients
\mathbb{Q}
whose Betti realisation has an underlying
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice, and those have a t-structure (
??
).

In general, we use a gluing procedure due to Vaish (
[
Vai20
]
) to derive a t-structure on a scheme
X
from the knowledge of a t-structure on fields. This is
??
and this gives an ordinary t-structure for integral Nori motives over any qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension. We denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
the heart of this t-structure.

In
[
Nor02
]
, Nori showed that
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})
is the derived category of constructible sheaves. Following his ideas and using the adaptation of his argument to the setting of rational Nori motives in
[
Tub25b
]
, we show that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}
is the derived category of ordinary Nori motives.

Theorem G

(
??
)
.

Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension. Then, the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence.

We also give two other applications of the ordinary t-structure: firstly, the Leray spectral sequence with integral coefficients is motivic
??
and secondly, we show that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
satisfies arc-hyperdescent over qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension
??
. We therefore expect it to be the case for
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
but we do not know how to prove it.

From the ordinary t-structure, we can construct a perverse t-structure using the usual gluing method of
[
BBD82
]
.

Proposition H

(
??
)
.

Let
X
be an excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension endowed with a dimension function. Then, there is a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
such that the
\ell
-adic realisation functor

R_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

is t-exact when the right-hand side is endowed with its perverse t-structure.

We let
{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
be the category of perverse Nori motives which is defined as the heart of the above t-structure. As in the case of perverse sheaves, the Verdier duality functor exchanges
{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
with another abelian category
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}^{+}(X,\mathbb{Z})
(see
??
). We can also define a motivic intermediate extension functor that is sent to its
\ell
-adic or analytic counterpart through the appropriate realisation functors (
??
). This gives a motivic intersection complex
\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. We prove that the classical pairings for intersection homology can be lifted to Nori motives. We expect that Wildeshaus’ intersection complex of Shimura varieties in étale motives
[
Wil12
,
Wil19
]
realises to the complex we defined though the functor from étale motives to Nori motives. Finally, any t-exactness property of the six functors which is true after realisation carries over to Nori motives, in particular, we can prove the hard Lefschetz theorem for Nori motives.

We then study whether
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is the derived category of perverse Nori motives. For this the classical argument of Beilinson
[
Bei87
]
fails because unipotent nearby cycles are badly behaved with integral coefficients. Under the assumption that the abelian category
{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
has
enough torsion-free objects
in the sense of
??
, it is easy to prove that the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence by reducing to torsion coefficients (this is Beilinson’s result) and to rational coefficients. Unfortunately, we were note able to prove that there are enough torsion-free objects, but we propose a strategy (
??
) that could work to achieve this goal. This is linked to a comparison with the approach of Ivorra quoted above, which has enough torsion-free objects because the generators of the category are very explicit.

The perverse t-structure on Nori motives can also be used to prove new cases of the Artin vanishing theorem for Artin motives proved by the first named author of the present paper in
[
Rui22
]
. Indeed, we prove in
??
that the functor from étale motives to Nori motives restricts to an equivalence on Artin motives, and we use that to show that if
f\colon X\to S
is a quasi-finite affine map of characteristic zero schemes, with regular source, the functor

f_{!}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathbb{Q}-c}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},sm0}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(S,\mathbb{Z})

is t-exact (see
??
). Here the left-hand side consists of
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible smooth Artin motives and the right-hand side consist of Artin motives; both sides are endowed with their perverse homotopy t-structure (these notions were defined by the first author in
[
Rui22
, Definition 3.2.1.1]
).

Integral Mixed Hodge modules.

The story we told also works for mixed Hodge modules, with simpler arguments. ,
[
Tub25b
]
and
[
Tub25a
]
provide an
\infty
-categorical enhancement of mixed Hodge modules, and a description of the objects of geometric origin as modules in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
over an algebra, as introduced by Drew in
[
Dre18
]
. Thus we can define a category of mixed Hodge modules with integral coefficients as the category of mixed Hodge modules with rational coefficients having an underlying lattice. We can also define these notions for schemes over the reals (by taking Galois homotopy fixed points), and prove that objects of geometric origin are also modules over an algebra in étale motives (
??
). Over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, this category contains the bounded derived category of mixed Hodge structures with integral coefficients (
??
). We can also prove that the stable
\infty
-category we define is indeed the bounded derived category of ordinary mixed modules with integral coefficients (
??
). Finally, we also prove in
??
that (admissible) variations of mixed Hodge structures with integral coefficients can be seen as integral mixed Hodge modules.

Conjectural picture.

One of the goal of this paper was to clarify how the existence of a motivic t-structure with rational coefficients would imply the existence of a motivic t-structure with integral coefficients.
Recall that following
[
Bei12
]
, by motivic t-structure on
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
we mean a t-structure compatible with the monoidal structure and such that the
\ell
-adic realisation functor

\rho_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{k}/k)))

(1)

is t-exact.
In
[
Tub25b
, Theorem 6.22]
the second author proved that if a motivic t-structure existed over schemes for rational motives, then étale motives and the indisation of the bounded derived category of perverse Nori motives would be equivalent. Using rigidity, we show in
??
that under the same assumption, étale motives with integral coefficients are also equivalent to Nori motives, so that they also afford a t-structure.
Moreover, we show that the motives killed by the Betti realisation are the only obstruction for
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})
to be the derived category of its heart (
??
).
This confirms a suggestion of Ayoub in
[
Ayo17b
, Remark 2.13]
, and implies that the following analogue of
[
Ayo17b
, Conjecture 2.8]
holds for Nori motives:

Proposition I

(
??
)
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. Let
M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
be a geometric Nori motive and let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})
be killed by the Betti realisation. Then the group

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{F},M)=0

vanishes.

Notations and conventions

We freely use the language of higher categories and higher algebra of
[
Lur09
,
Lur22
,
Lur
]
. We will denote by
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
the
\infty
-category of small
\infty
-categories,
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{St}}
) the
\infty
-category of presentable (resp. stable presentable)
\infty
-category,
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{perf}}
the
\infty
-category of stable and idempotent complete small
\infty
-categories, and
\Pr^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{\mathrm{St}}
the
\infty
-category of compactly generated stable
\infty
-categories. Those four
\infty
-categories are endowed with the Lurie tensor product, and the indisation functor
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}
is an equivalence between
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{perf}}
and
\Pr^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{\mathrm{St}}
, of inverse the functor sending a compactly generated
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
to its full subcategory
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
of compact objects.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category and
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})
is a commutative algebra object in
\mathcal{C}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
the
\infty
-category of
A
-modules in
\mathcal{C}
. If
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Sp}
is the
\infty
-category of spectra we omit
\mathrm{Sp}
from the notation:
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
. If
\mathcal{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category, and
a,b\in\mathcal{C}
,
we denote by
\mathrm{map}_{\mathcal{C}}(a,b)\in\mathrm{Sp}
the mapping spectrum between
a
and
b
. If
\Lambda
is an ordinary commutative ring, the abelian category of
\Lambda
-modules is denoted by
\Lambda
-
\mathrm{mod}
.

All schemes we consider are of characteristic zero, so that they have a map to
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
. If
X
is a scheme, we denote by
\mathrm{Sch}_{X}
the category of
X
-schemes, by
\mathrm{Sch}_{X}^{\mathrm{qcqs}}
the category of quasi-compact quasi-separated (which we will write qcqs in the rest of the paper)
X
-schemes, by
\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{ft}}_{X}
the category of
X
-schemes of finite type and by
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
the category of smooth separated
X
-schemes.

We will use
\infty
-categories of étale motives: if
X
is a scheme and
\Lambda
is a commutative ring, we denote by
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
the
\infty
-category of étale motives with coefficients in
\Lambda
: to construct it, one first considers
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant étale hypersheaves on the category
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
with values in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}
, and then one Tate-stabilise (see
[
Ayo14a
,
CD16
,
Rob14
]
). The thick subcategory generated by the Tate twisted image of the Yoneda functor

M_{X}\colon\mathrm{Sm}_{X}\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)

is
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
the
\infty
-category of motives of geometric origin (that is, it is the smallest category of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
closed under finite limits and colimits and by retracts that contains the
M_{X}(Y)(n)
for
Y\in\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
).

1 Preliminaries.

1.1 Computing right adjoints

Recall that an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
is called
rigid
if it is a stably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category in which every object is dualisable.

Proposition 1.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{c}
be a rigid stable
\infty
-category and denote by
\mathcal{E}
its indisation. Let
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}
be
\mathcal{E}
-modules in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
and let
\mathcal{A}
be an
\mathcal{E}
-algebra in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
which is also the indisation of a rigid
\infty
-category. Assume given an adjunction

F\colon\mathcal{M}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{N}\colon G

in which the right adjoint is co-continuous and the left adjoint is
\mathcal{E}
-linear. Then the right adjoint of

F\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}\colon\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{A}

is given by

G\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})
the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
of modules in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
over
\mathcal{E}
. By
[
HSS17
, Section 4.4]
this
\infty
-category has a natural
(\infty,2)
-categorical enhancement
\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})
such that the map
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{A}
of Ind-rigid categories induces an
2
-functor

-\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{A}\colon\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{A}).

Now, the adjunction

f^{*}\colon\mathcal{M}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{N}\colon f_{*}

is a priori only an adjunction internal to
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
. However, as
f^{*}
is
\mathcal{E}
-linear, the functor
f_{*}
has an
\mathcal{E}
-lax-linear structure for free (see
[
Lur22
, Examples 7.3.2.8 and 7.3.2.9]
). This lax structure is in fact strong by
[
HSS17
, Proposition 4.9 (3)]
, and as
f_{*}
is also a left adjoint, it is a map in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})
, so that by
[
BM24
, Proposition 5.2]
the adjunction
(f^{*},f_{*})
is an internal adjunction in
\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})
. As internal adjunctions are preserved by
2
-functors (because an internal adjunction in
\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})
is just a
2
-functor
\mathfrak{adj}\to\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{E})
from the ‘walking adjunction category’, see
[
Hau21
, Section 4]
), the image
(f^{*}\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}},f_{*}\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}})
of
(f_{*},f^{*})
by
-\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{A}
is again an internal adjunction. Thus, the right adjoint of
f^{*}\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}
is indeed
f_{*}\otimes_{\mathcal{E}}\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

∎

1.2 Lemmas on t-structures and bounded derived categories

For t-structures we will always use cohomological conventions as in
[
BBD82
, Section 1.3]
.

Lemma 1.2.1

(Lemma 3.2.18 of
[
RS20
]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C}_{i})_{i}
be a projective system of stable (resp. stable presentable)
\infty
-categories which admits a limit
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
(resp.
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{St}}
).
Assume that for any index
i
, the stable
\infty
-category
C_{i}
is endowed with a t-structure and such that all transition functors are t-exact.
Then,

\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 0}:=\{M\mid\forall i,p^{i}(M)\in\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\leqslant 0}\}

\mathcal{C}^{\geqslant 0}:=\{M\mid\forall i,p^{i}(M)\in\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\geqslant 0}\}

defines a t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
such that each projection
p^{i}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}_{i}
is t-exact.

Lemma 1.2.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{C}_{i})_{i}
be a filtered inductive system of stable
\infty
-categories which admits a colimit
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
Assume that for any index
i
, the stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{i}
is endowed with a t-structure and that all transition functors are t-exact.
Then,

\mathcal{C}^{\leqslant 0}:=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\leqslant 0}

\mathcal{C}^{\geqslant 0}:=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\geqslant 0}

defines a t-structure on
\mathcal{C}
such that each
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{C}
is t-exact.

Proof.

Filtered colimits in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
are computed in the category of simplicial sets. Thus, we can compute the mapping spaces as a colimit, and then as taking homotopy groups commutes with filtered colimits we obtain the orthogonality. The stability under shift is obvious, and the existence of triangles comes from the existence of triangle at some level of the colimit.
∎

If
\mathcal{A}
is an abelian category, we denote by
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
its bounded derived
\infty
-category as constructed in
[
BCKW19
, Section 7.4]
.

Lemma 1.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathcal{A}_{i}
be a filtered colimit of small abelian categories with exact transitions.
Then the canonical functor

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Denote by

F\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})

the canonical functor induced by the functors
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
. By
??
the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})
has a t-structure, of heart
\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathcal{A}_{i}=\mathcal{A}
. Thus, by the universal property of
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
proved in
[
BCKW19
, Corollary 7.4.12]
, there exists a canonical functor

G\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})

determined by its restriction to
\mathcal{A}
. The restriction of
F\circ G
to
\mathcal{A}
is the identity of
\mathcal{A}
, thus
F\circ G\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}
. Conversely, the functor
G\circ F
is determined by its composition with the natural functors
\iota_{j}\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{j})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})
which are themselves determined by their restriction to
\mathcal{A}_{j}
. The restriction to
\mathcal{A}_{j}
of
G\circ F\circ\iota_{j}
lands in

\mathcal{A}_{j}\subset\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathcal{A}_{i}\subset\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})

and is just the canonical functor
\mathcal{A}_{j}\to\mathcal{A}
, thus we have that
G\circ F\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}_{i})}
.
∎

Definition 1.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a small abelian category and let
n
be an integer. An object
A
of
\mathcal{A}
is

1.

torsion-free if for all nonzero integers
m\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
the map
A\xrightarrow{\times m}A
is a monomorphism.

2.

of
n
-torsion if the map
A\xrightarrow{\times n}A
is the zero map.

We denote by
\mathcal{A}[n]
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
that consists of
n
-torsion objects.

Finally, we say that
\mathcal{A}
has
enough torsion-free objects
if for any object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, there exists an epimorphism
B\twoheadrightarrow A
with
B
a torsion-free object.

For
A
a commutative ring, we denote by
\mathrm{Perf}_{A}
the
\infty
-category of perfect complexes of
A
-modules. It consists of dualisable objects of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.

Proposition 1.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a small abelian category and let
n
be a positive integer. Assume that
\mathcal{A}
has enough torsion-free objects.
Then the left derived functor

-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])

of the functor

-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\colon A\mapsto A/nA
exists, factors through the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}

and induces an fully faithful functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n]).

Proof.

The proof goes with several steps.

Step 1:
The functor

-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}[n]

is the left adjoint of the inclusion functor

\iota\colon\mathcal{A}[n]\to\mathcal{A},

and we can derive this adjunction to obtain an adjunction

-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])\colon\iota.

Indeed, the adjunction induces an adjunction of functors between the categories of bounded complexes
\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
and
\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])
and we can then use Cisinski’s formalism of derived functors: we endow
\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])
with the trivial structure of category with equivalences and fibrations (so that equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms and all maps are fibrations), and
\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
with the structure of a category with equivalences and cofibrations such that equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms and cofibrations are monomorphism with term wise torsion-free cokernels (this is indeed a structure of category with weak equivalences and cofibrations because there are enough torsion-free objects and that they are acyclic for
-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n
). By
[
Cis19
, Theorem 7.5.30]
, using that
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\simeq\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{A})[W_{qiso}^{-1}]
, we obtain the existence of the adjunction on the derived categories.

Step 2:
The functor
-\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
factors trough
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
. This is because of the universal property of the tensor product of idempotent complete categories: it is computed as the compact objects of the tensor product between indisation, and the latter has a universal property by definition of Lurie’s tensor product of presentable
\infty
-categories. As we have a
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])
and
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])
is
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
-linear, this step is clear.

Step 3:
The functor

\alpha\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])

is fully faithful.
Given two complexes
K,L
in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
and two complexes
P,Q
in
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}}(K\boxtimes P,L\boxtimes Q)\simeq\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(K,L)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}}(P,Q),

as it is proven in
[
HL23
, Proposition 3.5.5]
, where
K\boxtimes P
is the image of
(K,P)
under the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\times\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}.

To prove that
\alpha
is fully faithful, by dévissage it suffices to prove that it is fully faithful on objects of the form
A\boxtimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
with
A
an object of
\mathcal{A}
. The image of such an object in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])
is
A\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, given the above formula for the mapping spectra in the tensor product, we have to show that for two objects
A
and
B
of
\mathcal{A}
, the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(A,B)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])}(A\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},B\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence. This is true because

\displaystyle\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(A,B)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{cofib}(\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(A,B)\xrightarrow{\times n}\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(A,B))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(A,\mathrm{cofib}(B\xrightarrow{\times n}B))

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(A,\iota(B\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}[n])}(a\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},b\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{L}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),

where
(*)
can be checked by taking a torsion-free resolution of
b
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 1.2.6
.

The above functor in proposition is not essentially surjective in general, as it can be seen by taking
\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{ft}}
the abelian category of finitely generated abelian groups, where we get the difference between
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
and
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}^{\mathrm{ft}})
, where
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}^{\mathrm{ft}}
is the abelian category of finite type
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

1.3 Change of coefficients

For
\Lambda
a commutative ring, we denote by
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\Lambda}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
the
\infty
-category of
\Lambda
-linear presentable
\infty
-categories and by
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\Lambda}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{perf}})
the
\infty
-category of
\Lambda
-linear small idempotent-complete
\infty
-categories.

Lemma 1.3.1
.

Let
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a map in
\mathrm{CAlg}\left(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\right)
and let
G
be the right adjoint to
F
.
Assume that for any object
N
of
\mathcal{D}
, the natural map

G(N)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to G(N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})

is an equivalence. If
A
is a commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, denote by
F_{A}
the functor
F\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
.

Then, the functor
F
is fully faithful (
resp
. an equivalence) if and only if the functors
F_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
F_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
are fully faithful (
resp
. an equivalence) for any nonzero integer
n
.

Proof.

If
F
is fully faithful, then
[
Hai22
, Lemma 2.14]
ensures that
F_{A}
is for any commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. The same assertion about equivalences is obvious. This proves the "only if" part of the statement.

We now prove the converse.
Assume first that the functors
F_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
F_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
are fully faithful. Let
M
be an object of
\mathcal{C}
. We want to prove that the natural map

M\to GF(M)

is an equivalence. This can be checked after tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
and with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Since tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
commutes with any exact functor, the map
M/n\to GF(M)/n
being an equivalence amounts to the full faithfulness of
F_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
.

On the other hand, our assumption on
G
implies that the right adjoint of
F_{\mathbb{Q}}
sends an object of the form
N\otimes\mathbb{Q}
to
G(N\otimes\mathbb{Q})
. Therefore, the full faithfulness of
F_{\mathbb{Q}}
implies that the map

M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to GF(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is an equivalence. Hence, the functor
F
is fully faithful.

Assume now further that
F_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
F_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
are essentially surjective. Let
N
be an object of
\mathcal{D}
. Then, we have an exact triangle

N\to N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\ N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z};

the essential surjectivity of
F_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
F_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
imply that
N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
and
N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
belong to the image of
F
. As
F
is fully faithful, the latter is closed under colimits and finite limits, so that
N
belongs to it. This finishes the proof
∎

Remark 1.3.2
.

An obvious case where the previous
??
applies is when
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is a functor between compactly generated presentable
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear
\infty
-categories that preserves compact objects. Indeed, in that case the right adjoint to
F
commutes with colimits hence also with

(\mathrm{Id}\to-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}(\mathrm{Id}\xrightarrow{n}\mathrm{Id}).

However, we will apply this lemma in a case where
\mathcal{C}
is
not
compactly generated.

We will also need to consider torsion objects in an
\infty
-category.

Definition 1.3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an object of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}})
or
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}})
and let
p
be a prime number. An object
M
is

1.

torsion when
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}=0
.

2.

p
-torsion when
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[1/p]=0
.

3.

p
-complete when the canonical map
M\to\lim M/p^{n}
is an equivalence.

We denote by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{tors}}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{p-\mathrm{tors}}
, resp.
\mathcal{C}^{\wedge}_{p}
) the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
made of its torsion (resp.
p
-torsion, resp.
p
-complete) objects.

In the case when
\mathcal{C}
belongs to
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}})
, the inclusion
\mathcal{C}^{\wedge}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
has a left adjoint
(-)^{\wedge}_{p}
given by
M\mapsto\lim M/p^{n}
called the
p
-completion. Furthermore, the map
(-)^{\wedge}_{p}
induces an equivalence

\mathcal{C}_{p-\mathrm{tors}}\to\mathcal{C}^{\wedge}_{p}

with an inverse given by
M\mapsto M\otimes\mathbb{Z}(p^{\infty})
where
\mathbb{Z}(p^{\infty})\subseteq\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
denotes the Prüfer
p
-group.

Given a map
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}})
, we can also construct its
p
-completion
F^{\wedge}_{p}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\wedge}_{p}\to\mathcal{D}^{\wedge}_{p}
which sends
M=\lim M/p^{n}
to
\lim F(M)/p^{n}
.

2 Derived categories of Nori motives over a field.

2.1 Reminders on Nori motives

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. Nori constructed a symmetric monoidal abelian category
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
that affords the following universal property (see
[
HMS17
, Section 9 and Corollary 10.1.6]
):
there is a cohomological functor

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})

factoring the
\mathrm{H}^{0}
of the Betti realisation

\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}

of
[
Ayo10
]
which is universal for this property: any cohomological functor

\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{C}

to a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear abelian category
\mathcal{C}
, such that there exists a faithful exact functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathbb{Z}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}
giving a factorisation of the
\mathrm{H}^{0}
of the Betti realisation of étale motives, will factor uniquely through
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{N}}
, in a way compatible with the various Betti realisations.

The definition also work with rational coefficients. In this case, Nori motives form a tannakian category:

Theorem 2.1.1

(Nori,
[
HMS17
, Theorem 9.1.5]
)
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. The Betti realisation

\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{Vect}^{\mathrm{fd}}_{\mathbb{Q}}

of Nori motives is a fiber functor of Tannakian categories. In particular, there exists a Hopf algebra
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}\in\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that the Betti realisation factors as an equivalence

\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{coMod}_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}}(\mathrm{Vect}^{\mathrm{fd}}_{\mathbb{Q}})

where
\mathrm{coMod}_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}}(\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathrm{fd}})
is the abelian categories of comodules over
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
in the category
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathrm{fd}}
of finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces.

We will need a result about torsion Nori motives. As Artin motives embed fully faithfully in
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
and the Betti realisation on those is conservative and t-exact, the functor

\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{N}}:\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})

induces a faithful exact functor

\iota\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{k}/k),\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})

(2.1.A)

where
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{k}/k),\mathbb{Z})
is the abelian category of
\mathbb{Z}
-linear continuous representations of
\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{k}/k)
which are finitely generated as
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

Theorem 2.1.2

(Nori
[
Fak00
]
)
.

The functor
\iota
defined above induces an equivalence

\iota\colon\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{Gal}(\bar{k}/k),\mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}

on the full subcategories of torsion objects.

Proof.

The proof can be found in
[
Fak00
, Theorem 6.1]
, but see also the proof of
??
which is a generalisation.
∎

2.2 Realisation functor to the derived category of ind Nori motives.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. In this section, we recall how to construct a realisation functor

\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})),

compatible with the Betti realisation.
Its construction goes back to Nori and Beilinson’s Basic Lemma, and we will use the description given in
[
CG17
]
.

Construction 2.2.1
.

By
[
CG17
, Proposition 7.1 and its proof]
, there is
a lax symmetric monoidal
1
-functor

\mathrm{SmAff}_{k}\to\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})^{\mathrm{eff}})

from the category of affine
k
-schemes to the category of bounded complexes of effective Nori motives (
[
HMS17
, Definition 9.1.3]
).
We can compose this functor with the
functor

\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})^{\mathrm{eff}})\to\mathrm{Ch}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))

given by the map
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})^{\mathrm{eff}}\to\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.
The construction roughly goes as follows:
for each smooth affine variety
X
, one can construct (using Nori’s Basic Lemma) a finite
cellular filtration

\mathcal{F}
on
X
by closed subschemes which computes the cohomology of
X

(this is similar to how the cellular filtration of a CW-complex computes its cohomology).
This gives a bounded complex
C_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}(X)
in
\mathrm{Ch}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
whose Betti realisation computes the singular cohomology of the variety of the complex points of
X
.
To make a functor out of it, one has to make choices to have cellular filtrations compatible with morphisms;
to that end, one considers the colimit
C^{*}(X)
(in
\mathrm{Ch}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
) over all possible cellular filtrations.
Moreover, the composition
R_{\mathrm{B}}\circ C^{*}
with the Betti realisation
functor
R_{\mathrm{B}}
is naturally isomorphic (as a monoidal functor)
to the functor

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}\colon X\mapsto C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}(X^{\mathrm{an}})

sending a
k
-variety to the complex of singular
chains over the analytic variety of its complex points (this is
[
CG17
, Corollary 6.4]
).
Notice finally that the functor
C^{*}
sends étale
k
-schemes to objects of
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
seen as complexes placed in degree
0
(where it is given by the functor
\iota
of
??
).

We now use Blumberg, Gepner and Tabuda’s
[
BGT14
, Proposition 3.5]
.
First notice that

C^{*}\colon\mathrm{SmAff}_{k}\to\mathrm{Ch}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))

is a lax
symmetric monoidal functor between symmetric monoidal
1
-categories.
Moreover, by construction of the functor
C^{*}
, we have
C^{*}(k)=\mathbb{Z}_{k}

and for any affine
k
-varieties
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, the canonical map

C^{*}(X_{1})\otimes C^{*}(X_{2})\to C^{*}(X_{1}\times X_{2})

is a quasi-isomorphism, because this is true when
C^{*}
is replaced by

C^{*}_{\mathrm{sing}}
.
Setting the weak equivalences on
\mathrm{SmAff}_{k}
to be the isomorphisms of
schemes, and the class of weak equivalences
W_{\mathrm{qiso}}
in

\mathrm{Ch}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
to be the class of quasi-isomorphisms,
the functor
C^{*}
preserves weak equivalences. Therefore, the
proposition quoted above yields
a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
and a symmetric
monoidal functor

\mathrm{SmAff}_{k}^{\times}\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))^{\otimes}

whose underlying functor is

\mathrm{SmAff}_{k}\xrightarrow{C^{*}}\mathrm{Ch}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\xrightarrow{W_{\mathrm{qiso}}^{-1}}\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})).

Now, by
[
CG17
, Proposition 7.1]
the above functor factors through étale motives. If
K/k
is an extension of subfields of
\mathbb{C}
, then as any cellular filtration of a variety over
k
provides a cellular filtration of its base change to
K
, this functor is compatible with change of fields. Moreover, by
[
CG17
, Proposition 7.1]
, the composition of
C^{*}
with the Betti realisation of Nori motives is equivalent to the Betti realisation of étale motives.
Thus we obtained:

Theorem 2.2.2

(Choudhury-Gallauer, Harrer, Nori)
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
.
There exists a symmetric monoidal functor

\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))

that gives back the Betti realisation when composed with the Betti realisation of Nori motives, and sends

\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
to
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
. Moreover, the construction is compatible with pullback of fields.

It will be crucial for us that, rationnally, the above realisation functor is in some sense compatible with the realisation functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

of
[
Tub25b
]
which is given by the universal property of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
as a six functors formalism.

We begin with a lemma.

Lemma 2.2.3
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. The canonical functor

\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

induces an equivalence
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
where the tensor product is taken in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
.

Proof.

By
[
Lur
, C.4.2.2]
, the functor
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is the stabilisation of
the functor

\mathrm{D}^{\leqslant 0}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\otimes\mathrm{Mod}^{\leqslant 0}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{D}^{\leqslant 0}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})).

Now, recall (
[
HMS17
, Definition 7.1.10]
) that
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
is a colimit of categories of finitely generated modules over some rings
E_{F}
, so that
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})

is in fact the filtered colimit of
\mathrm{Mod}_{E_{F}}^{\heartsuit}
. Thus, the prestable category
\mathrm{D}^{\leqslant 0}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
is the filtered colimit of the prestable categories

\mathrm{Mod}_{E_{F}}^{\leqslant 0}
as the functor

\mathrm{D}^{\leqslant 0}(-)
is a left adjoint by
[
Lur
, C.5.4.9]
. Finally, the tensor product with
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\leqslant 0}
above is the filtered colimit
of
\mathrm{Mod}_{E_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}^{\leqslant 0}
, but this is
\mathrm{D}^{\leqslant 0}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
by
[
HMS17
, Lemma 7.2.2 1.]
.
∎

We now prove that when rationalised, the funtor
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
is very close to the functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

constructed by the second author in
[
Tub25b
]
.
We begin with some reminders on weak Tanakian formalism from a monadic point of view. The next propositions consist of bookeeping Lurie’s higher algebra book and Raksit’s
[
Rak26
]
to revisit the constructions of Ayoub
[
Ayo14b
]
in the setting of
\infty
-categories.

Definition 2.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.

1.

A commutative bi-algebra in
\mathcal{C}
is an object of
\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})):=\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}
, that is an object of the opposite category of associative algebras in the opposite category of commutative algebras in
\mathcal{C}
.

2.

A Hopf algebra is a bi-algebra
H
in
\mathcal{C}
equiped with a homotopy antipode, that is the bi-algebra
\mathrm{ho}(H)\in\mathrm{ho}(\mathcal{C})
is a Hopf algebra in the usual sense.

Remark 2.2.5
.

By
[
Rak26
, Proposition 2.1.2]
, there is a natural equivalence
\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}))\simeq\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C})):=\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Alg}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}})
. In particular, any commutative bi-algebra
A
has an underlying co-algebra structure in
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 2.2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category and let
A
be a commutative co-algebra in
\mathcal{C}
. The
\infty
-category of comodules over
A
is by definition

\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C}):=\mathrm{LMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}

where we see
A
as an associative algebra in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
through
??
. By
[
Rak26
, Proposition 2.2.1]
(see
??
and also
[
Bea23
, Theorem 3.18]
), the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
is naturally symmetric monoidal and the forgetful functor
\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
is symmetric monoidal.

Remark 2.2.7
.

Recall that a functor
F\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
is
comonadic
if it has a right adjoint
G
such that the natural factorisation through modules over the endomorphism comonad (
[
Lur22
, Proposition 4.7.3.3]
)

\mathcal{D}\to\mathrm{coMod}_{T}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C},

where
T
is the opposite of the endomorphism monad
T_{F^{\mathrm{op}}}\in\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}))
of
F^{\mathrm{op}}
and
\mathrm{coMod}_{T}(\mathcal{C}):=\mathrm{LMod}_{T_{F^{\mathrm{op}}}}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}
, induces an equivalence
\mathcal{D}\to\mathrm{coMod}_{T}(\mathcal{C})
. The typical example is the above
\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
.

Lemma 2.2.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category and let
H
be a Hopf algebra in
\mathcal{C}
. Consider the co-bar construction
H_{\bullet}:=\mathrm{coBar}(H)\colon\Delta\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})_{/\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}}}
(
[
Lur22
, Construction 4.4.2.7]
). Let

\mathbf{coMod}_{H}(\mathcal{C}):=\lim_{n\in\Delta}\mathrm{Mod}_{H_{n}}(\mathcal{C}).

Then the canonical functor
o_{H}\colon\mathbf{coMod}_{H}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
given by the projection on
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}}}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathcal{C}
is comonadic, and induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\mathbf{coMod}_{H}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathrm{coMod}_{H}(\mathcal{C}).

Proof.

See
[
Gai15
, Proposition E.1.4]
(this is a direct application of
[
Lur22
, Theorem 4.7.5.2]
).
∎

Recall the following result on endomorphism comonads:

Theorem 2.2.9

(
[
Lur22
, §4.7.3]
,
[
Hau21
, Corollary 5.12]
)
.

The forgetful functor

\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{\mathrm{comnd}\text{-}\mathrm{ladj}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{\mathrm{ladj}}

from comonadic left adjoints functors to left adjoints functors admits a left adjoint sending a left adjoint
F:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
to the forgetful functor
\mathrm{coMod}_{(T_{F^{\mathrm{op}}})^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}
from comodules over the endomorphism comonad.

When considering linear
\infty
-categories over a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, the above adjunction simplifies drastically.

Proposition 2.2.10

(Raksit)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. The association
A\mapsto\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C}):=\mathrm{LMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}
assembles to give a symmetric monoidal functor

\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathcal{C}}

from co-algebras in
\mathcal{C}
to right
\mathcal{C}
-modules in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, with a map to
\mathcal{C}
(that is, pairs
(\mathcal{M},U)
with
\mathcal{M}
an
\infty
-category with a
\mathcal{C}
-module structure, and
U\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{C}
a
\mathcal{C}
-linear functor). The symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C})
is inherited from that of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
and the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathcal{C}}
is inherited from that of
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, which is the cartesian one.

Then this functor is fully faithful and its image consists of pairs
(\mathcal{M},U)
such that
U
is comonadic and the right adjoint of
U
is
\mathcal{C}
-linear.
Moreover, consider the full subcategory

\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathcal{C}}^{0}\subset\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathcal{C}}

consisting of pairs
(\mathcal{M},U)
where the right adjoint of
U
is also
\mathcal{C}
-linear. Then the induced functor

\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{/\mathcal{C}}^{0}

admits a left adjoint
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

The existence of the functor is
[
Rak26
, Proposition 2.2.1]
, the full faithfullness is
[
Rak26
, Proposition 2.3.6]
, and the existence of the left adjoint
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
follows from
[
Rak26
, Lemma 2.3.5]
(see the proof of
[
Rak26
, Proposition 2.3.6]
).
∎

Remark 2.2.11
.

Under the equivalence
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathcal{C}
between
\mathcal{C}
-linear endofunctors of
\mathcal{C}
and the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
itself (the functor is given by evalutation at
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}}
, see for example
[
Lur22
, Corollary 4.8.5.21]
applied to the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{C}
), the functor
\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
of
??
is induced by that of
??
. In particular, the co-multiplication of the algebra
A=\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{M},U)
, for
U\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{C}
a
\mathcal{C}
-linear functor whose right adjoint
G
is also
\mathcal{C}
-linear is given by

A=UG(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{unit}}U(GU)G(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}})=UG\Big((UG)(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}})\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}}\Big)\simeq UG(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}})\otimes UG(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{C}})=A\otimes A

where in the last equivalence we use the
\mathcal{C}
-linearity of
G
and
U
.

Corollary 2.2.12
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. The functor
\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
induces a fully faithful right adjoint functor

\mu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C}))\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})^{0}_{\mathcal{C}//\mathcal{C}}

from commutative co-algebras in
\mathcal{C}
to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})^{0}_{\mathcal{C}//\mathcal{C}}
of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{M}
with a symmetric monoidal functor
U\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
that has a symmetric monoidal section
s\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{M}
and such that the underlying right adjoint of
U
is
\mathcal{C}
-linear.

Proof.

Because
\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
is symmetric monoidal, it induces a functor betwen commutative algebras. The identification of the commutative algebras objects in
\mathrm{RMod}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})^{0}_{/\mathcal{C}}
is explained in
[
Rak26
, Construction 2.2.2]
. We have to check that the functor
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
induces a left adjoint to
\mu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}}
. Because
\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
is symmetric monoidal, the functor
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
is oplax symmetric monoidal, thus it suffices to check that the canonical map
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{M}_{2},U_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{C}}U_{2})\to\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{M}_{1},U_{1})\otimes\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{M}_{2},U_{2})
is an equivalence. By construction of
\nu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}
and by definition of the relative tensor product as the geometric realisation of the bar construction, it suffices to check that if
T_{i}\in\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}))^{\mathrm{op}}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
are two comonads, then the natural map
\mathrm{coMod}_{T_{1}\times T_{2}}(\mathcal{C}_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{2})\to\mathrm{coMod}_{T_{1}}(\mathcal{C}_{1})\times\mathrm{coMod}_{T_{2}}(\mathcal{C}_{2})
, induced by the left adjoint of the product preserving functor of
??
, is an equivalence. This follows directly from
[
Lur22
, Corollary 4.7.3.16]
as both forgetful functors to
\mathcal{C}_{1}\times\mathcal{C}_{2}
are comonadic with the same comonad.
∎

With this in mind, we can enhance
[
Ayo14b
, Section 1]
to
\infty
-categories.

Proposition 2.2.13
.

Let
R\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal left adjoint functor between symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories. Assume the following:

1.

R
admits a section
e\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{M}
which is symmetric monoidal.

2.

The right adjoint
R_{*}
of
R
is
\mathcal{M}
-linear, that is, for every
c\in\mathcal{C}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
, the natural map

R_{*}(c)\otimes m\to R_{*}(c\otimes R(m))

is an equivalence.

Then there exists a Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}\in\mathcal{C}
and a factorisation

\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathrm{univ}}}\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{o_{\mathcal{H}}}\mathcal{C}

of
R
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})
with the following property:
For any commutative bi-algebra
A\in\mathcal{C}
and any symmetric monoidal left adjoint functor

\rho\colon\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})^{\otimes}

whose composition with the forgetful functor is
R
, there exists an essentially unique map
\mathcal{H}\to A
of commutative bi-algebras such that the following diagram commutes:
