{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{59.8234pt}{-21.98378pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 82.78 -30.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Proof.

The hypothesis on
R
makes the datum
(\mathcal{M},R)
an object of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})^{0}_{\mathcal{C}//\mathcal{C}}
: the
\mathcal{M}
-linearity of
R_{*}
together with the existence of the section
e
implies that
R_{*}
is
\mathcal{C}
-linear. Let
\nu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}}
be the right adjoint of
\mu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}}
, and let
\mathcal{H}:=\nu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{M},R)\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C}))
. By construction, the homotopy bi-algebra
\mathrm{ho}(\mathcal{H})
is exactly the same as the one considered in
[
Ayo14b
, Théorème 1.21]
. In particular, as our hypothesis on
R
imply
[
Ayo14b
, Hypothèse 1.40]
, there exists a homotopy antipode by
[
Ayo14b
, Théorème 1.45]
. Thus,
\mathcal{H}
is a Hopf algebra in
\mathcal{C}
. Moreover, the unit of the adjunction
(\nu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}},\mu^{\otimes}_{\mathcal{C}})
provides a factorisation of
R
as

\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathrm{univ}}}\mathrm{coMod}_{H}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathcal{C}

where all
\infty
-categories are symmetric monoidal and the functors symmetric monoidal and
\mathcal{C}
-linear.

Let
A\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C}))
be a commutative bi-algebra. Then using the adjunction, we have:

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{coAlg}(\mathcal{C}))}(\mathcal{H},A)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})_{\mathcal{C}//\mathcal{C}}}((\mathcal{M},R),(\mathrm{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C}),U)).

This provides the wanted universal property. The fact that in the proposition we used
\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{C})
instead of
\mathrm{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{C})
is allowed by
??
.

∎

Remark 2.2.14
.

The comodule structure on
R(M)
provided by the factorization through
\rho_{\mathrm{univ}}
is nothing more than the co-unit
R(M)\xrightarrow{R(\varepsilon)}RR_{*}R(M)\simeq R(M)\otimes R(R_{*}(\mathbbm{1}))
, where
R_{*}
is the right adjoint of
R
. The functor from comodules over
\mathcal{H}
to comodules over
H
is also induced by the adjunction
(\rho,\rho_{*})
. In particular, we see that up to forget some structure, we recover the factorisation of
[
Ayo14b
, Lemme 1.55]
.

We now specify to motives. When using
??
, we may choose a bigger universe so that our presentable
\infty
-categories become small.

Lemma 2.2.15
.

Consider the Betti realisation
\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})
. Then using the essentially unique symmetric monoidal left adjoint
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, the couple
(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q}),\rho_{\mathrm{B}})
satifies the hypothesis of
??
.

Proof.

We have to check that the right adjoint of
\rho_{\mathrm{B}}
is
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
-linear. This follows from
[
HSS17
, Proposition 4.9]
: the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is compactly generated (see for instance
[
EHIK21b
, Lemma 5.1]
) and that its compact objects are the geometric motives, and the latter are dualisable over a field (
[
VSF00
, Chapter 5, Theorem 4.3.7]
).
∎

Recollection 2.2.16
.

Consider the Betti realisation
\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})
. In
[
Ayo14b
]
, Ayoub endows the object
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}:=\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\rho_{\mathrm{B}*}(\mathbb{Q})
with the structure of a Hopf algebra in
\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))
(here
\rho_{\mathrm{B}*}
is the right adjoint of
\rho_{\mathrm{B}})
. By
??
and
??
, this enhances to a Hopf algebra structure in
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})
and we can factor
\rho_{\mathrm{B}}
by a symmetric monoidal functor
\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{B}}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))
and the forgetful functor of comodules.

Assume now we have a Hopf algebra
H
in
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})
and a symmetric monoidal left adjoint functor

\rho_{H}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbf{coMod}_{H}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))

factoring the Betti realisation through the forgetful functor
o_{H}
. Applying
??
we obtain a unique map
\mathcal{H}_{A}\to H
of Hopf algebras such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{coMod}_{H}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}_{A}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cores}}

of symmetric monoidal left adjoints commutes.

Finally, if
H
is concentrated in degre zero, then by
[
GR17
, Proposition 2.4.3]
, there is a natural functor

\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{coMod}_{H}(\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}))\to\mathbf{coMod}_{H}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))

which induces an equivalence between left bounded complexes (so that its restriction to
\mathrm{D}^{+}
is fully faithful).

Remark 2.2.17
.

Using the universal property of
\mathcal{H}
(
??
), the universal property of the motivic Galois group of Iwanari in
[
Iwa18
]
, and the fact that
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{H})
coincides with Iwanari’s motivic Galois group (
[
Ayo22
, Remark 1.3.22]
), it is not hard to check that the two possible universal functors
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))
defined using the two universal properties agree.

However, in
[
Ayo26
,
Ayo17a
]
, Ayoub enhances
\mathcal{H}
to a stronger version of Hopf algebra (that is,
\mathcal{H}
is a group object in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))^{\mathrm{op}}
) and constructs a symmetric monoidal realisation functor
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))
. We did not try comparing this functor with the one we just constructed.

With the above, we can interpret the main theorem of
[
CG17
]
as a description of the universal property of
\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{B}}}
:

Construction 2.2.18
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. By
[
Hub93
, Proposition 2.2]
, the functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is fully faithful thus using
??
we can see
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
as the full subcategory of
\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))
consisting of objects whose underlying object
o_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}}
is a perfect complex. Consider the functor

\rho_{\mathrm{CG}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

obtained by restricting to
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
the functor
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
of
??
(where we identify the target using
??
). This induces a functor

\rho_{\mathrm{CG}}^{\mathrm{big}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathrm{H}_{N}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})).

It factors the Betti realisation, thus, by
??
, this induces a map of Hopf algebras
\varphi\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
. By
[
Ayo14b
, Corollary 2.105]
, the object
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}
is connective in
\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})
, thus as
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
is concentrated in degree zero, the map
\varphi
factors trough the co-connective cover
\tau\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{A}}
, giving a map
\overline{\varphi}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{A}}:=\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}})\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
, which is a map of Hopf algebras (this is because
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}
is connective).

Theorem 2.2.19

(Choudhury-Gallauer)
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. The map
\overline{\varphi}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
of
??
is an equivalence of Hopf algebras. In particular, the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{CG}}^{\mathrm{big}}
fits in the following commutative diagram of symmetric monoidal left adjoint functors:

]

∎

We now prove the expected comparison result.

Proposition 2.2.20
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. Then the composition

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathrm{N}}}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}

is naturally isomorphic to the functor
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
constructed in
??
tensored by
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}}
.

Proof.

First, by functoriality of the Lurie tensor product, the composition of
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}}
with the functor

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is the functor obtained out of the universal property of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
with
C^{*}
replaced by
C^{*}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Moreover as
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is compactly generated, it suffices to show that the two functors coincide when restricted to
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
.

We now apply
??
to the realisation functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\mathrm{big}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbf{coMod}_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}}(\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q}))
obtained by extending by colimits the functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

constructed in
[
Tub25b
]
using the 6 operations. This provides a Hopf algebra morphism
\alpha\colon\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}}\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
, that we can factor as
\alpha\simeq\overline{\alpha}\circ\tau
with
\overline{\alpha}\colon\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{A}})\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}}
. Moreover, using that
\rho_{\mathrm{CG}}\simeq\mathrm{cores}_{\overline{\varphi}}\circ\mathrm{cores}_{\tau}\circ\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{B}}}
(
??
), we have a commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

By
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 6.20]
, the universal factorisation functor
\mathrm{H}^{0}_{\mathrm{N}}
of
??
is the composition
\mathrm{H}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
. As this is also true for
\mathrm{H}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{CG}}
(see the proof of
[
HMS17
, Corollary 10.1.7]
), by the universal property of Nori motives (
[
IM24
, Section 1]
), this implies that
\mathrm{cores}_{\overline{\alpha}(\overline{\varphi}^{-1})}\colon\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is the identity functor. By
[
BCKW19
, Corollary 7.4.12]
, the t-exactness also implies that the functor on
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is the identity functor, finishing the proof.
∎

2.3 The algebra representing Nori motivic cohomology.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. Thanks to
??
we have at hand a symmetric monoidal functor

\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))

compatible with the Betti realisation.

Definition 2.3.1
.

Let
F
be the right adjoint of
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
. We denote by
\mathscr{N}_{k}
the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring object in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
given by applying the lax symmetric monoidal functor
F
to
the unit object
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
of
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
.

Lemma 2.3.2
.

Let
M\in\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
. Then the canonical map

F(M)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to F(M\otimes\mathbb{Q})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that for any smooth
k
-scheme
X
and any integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(M_{k}(X)(i),F(M)\otimes\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(M_{k}(X)(i),F(M\otimes\mathbb{Q}))

is an equivalence. Using that, for constructible étale motives, tensorisation by the rationals can be set out of the mapping spectra (see for example
[
CD16
, Corollary 5.4.9]
), and using the adjunction, we see that it suffices to prove that
the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i)),M)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i)),M\otimes\mathbb{Q})

is an equivalence.
The right-hand side can be rewritten as

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i))\otimes\mathbb{Q},M\otimes\mathbb{Q})

thus using
??
, the above map is in fact

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i)),M)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i))\otimes\mathbb{Q},M\otimes\mathbb{Q})

which is an equivalence by
[
BVK16
, Proposition B.4.1]
.
∎

Let
k\subset K
be subfields of
\mathbb{C}
and denote by
f\colon\operatorname{Spec}(K)\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
the associated map of schemes. By construction we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(K,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(K,\mathbb{Z}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}

in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
. It induces an exchange transformation between
f^{*}
and
F
.
In particular, we have a map

f^{*}\mathscr{N}_{k}\to\mathscr{N}_{K}

(2.3.A)

of algebras in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(K,\mathbb{Z})
. We will prove that this map is an equivalence, but first we need two important propositions:

Proposition 2.3.3
.

Let
\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}
be the algebra constructed in
[
Tub25b
]
, that is the algebra obtained by applying the right adjoint of the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
to the unit object.
By
??
and
??
we have a natural ring map

\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathscr{N}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
. This map is an equivalence.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that the map is an equivalence after applying the functors

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(M_{k}(X)(i),-)\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})

for
X
a smooth
k
-scheme and
i
an integer. Using adjunctions and
??
,
we see that it suffices to check that the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\rho_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i)),\mathbb{Q}_{k})\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i)),\mathbb{Q}_{k})

is an equivalence, which is the case because on
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
the functor

\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

is fully faithful.
∎

Recall that the chang of site from the small étale site to the smooth étale site induces a functor

\iota\colon\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},R)\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,R)

for any commutative ring
R
and any scheme
X
. By the rigidity theorem (see
[
Bac21
]
for the most general statement), this functor is an equivalence when
R
is a torsion ring.

Proposition 2.3.4
.

The ring map
\mathbbm{1}_{k}\to\mathscr{N}_{k}
in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
becomes an equivalence after passing mod
n
for any nonzero integer
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
:

\mathbbm{1}_{k}/n\simeq\mathscr{N}_{k}/n
.

Proof.

Again to prove this, it suffices to prove that for every smooth
k
-scheme
X
and any integer
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(M_{k}(X)(i),\mathbbm{1}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}(k,\mathbb{Z})}(M_{k}(X)(i),\mathscr{N}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence. Using adjunctions and rigidity for étale motives, together with the fact that tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is a finite colimit thus goes out of the mapping spectra,
it suffices to prove that the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(k_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})}(\iota^{-1}(M_{k}(X)(i)),\mathbbm{1}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))}(\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}(M_{k}(X)(i)),\mathbb{Z}_{k})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}

is an equivalence. The right-hand side is in fact by
??
(that we can use because
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
has enough torsionfree objects) the mapping complex in the category

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})[n])
. By
??
the category
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})[n]
is the category of finite type representations of the absolute Galois group of
k
with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
coefficients.
In particular,
\mathrm{D}^{b}(k_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
embeds in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})[n])
, its image being exactly complexes of representations with perfect underlying complex of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-modules. This finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.3.5
.

Let
k\subset K
be a map of subfield of the complex numbers. The natural map

(\mathscr{N}_{k})_{\mid K}\to\mathscr{N}_{K}
of
??
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Indeed, it suffices to prove this after rationalisation and passing mod
n
for all nonzeros integers
n
. The rational statement is proven in
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 6.16]
, and the torsion statement follows from the fact that
f^{*}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
for all
f
, as any exact functor commutes with cofibers.
∎

We finish this section with the following result:

Theorem 2.3.6
.

Let
k
be a subfield of the complex numbers. Consider the factorisation of
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
through the modules category

\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{k}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})).

This functor is fully faithful when restricted to the thick subcategory
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
of
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Z}):=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{k}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
generated by the
M_{k}(X)(i)\otimes\mathscr{N}_{k}
for
X
smooth over
k
and induces an equivalence between the latter and
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
is an idempotent complete stable
\infty
-category, also note that its image through
\overline{\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}}
is contained in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
because the image of the
M_{k}(X)
through
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
belongs to
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
.

We first prove that

\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}
is fully faithful when restricted to
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.
To do so, it suffices to check that it is the case after being tensored over
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}
by

\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
for all nonzero integers
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
When tensored by
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, using
??
and
[
Tub25b
, Proposition 6.16]
we see that the functor is fully faithful.
When tensored by
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
, this follows from
??
,
??
,
??
and rigidity for étale motives.
We now prove essential surjectivity. In fact, the proof of fully faithfulness rationally gives that the functor

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))

is an equivalence when tensored by
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. In particular, if
K\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
is a complex of Nori motives, we see that there exist an object

P\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
and an map
\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}(P)\to M
that becomes an equivalence after being tensored by
\mathbb{Q}
. Said in an other way,
this map
\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}(P)\to M
has torsion cofibre, thus we may assume that
M
is torsion: there exists an integer
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that the multiplication by
n
on
M
is zero.
In this case we have that
M\oplus M[1]\simeq M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, so it suffices to reach
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.
But because
M
is dualisable, the image
M\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
of
M
in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})[n])
has perfect underlying complex of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-modules, thus lies in
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(k_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
. By rigidity of étale motives again, we
see that
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is in the image of
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}
and therefore in that of
\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrm{N}}}
, finishing the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.3.7
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. Then the thick category
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
of
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Z})
generated by the
M_{k}(X)(i)\otimes\mathscr{N}_{k}
for
X
smooth over
k
is endowed with a t-structure such that the Betti realisation is t-exact.

Remark 2.3.8
.

The above construction of
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Z})=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{k}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
is a bit more involved that what the reader might have expected; there are indeed two naive possibilities for a presentable
\infty
-category of Nori motives over
k
: one could take the unbounded derived category
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
of the indisation of Nori motives, or take the indization
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
of the bounded derived category of Nori motives. However, they do not give the correct presentable category because they cannot coincide with
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.

More precisely, it is believed that the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
affords a t-structure, that restricts to the subcategory
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
whose heart would then be
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
. But then, the above naive definitions cannot give the right answer: firstly, it is known by a counterexample of Ayoub (
[
Ayo17b
, Lemma 2.4]
) that the Betti realisation of the big category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is not conservative, so that it is not possible in general (at least if
k
is of infinite transcendence degree over
\mathbb{Q}
) that this category is of the form
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
because the Betti realisation of the latter
is
conservative; secondly, the category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
is not compactly generated by
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
when the field
k
has infinite cohomological dimension (take
k=\mathbb{R}
the field of real numbers), so that
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z}))
cannot be the right answer either. However see
??
for a result related to this discussion.

3 Relative Nori motives.

3.1 Relative Nori motives as modules in étale motives

In
[
Tub25b
]
it was proven that the construction of Ivorra and Morel in
[
IM24
]
has a natural interpretation in terms of modules in étale motives. Indeed, if for
X
a finite type
k
-scheme, one denote by

\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Q}):=\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}({\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}}(X))

the indization of the bounded derived category of perverse motives, the realisation functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Q})

factors through
the
\infty
-category of modules in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
over the algebra
\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}:=\pi_{X}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}
, with
\pi_{X}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
the structural morphism, and induces an equivalence with the latter and
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Q})
: there is a natural equivalence of functors

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{(-)}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q}).

In the previous section, we constructed a commutative algebra
\mathscr{N}_{k}
in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
which is equivalent to
\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}
when rationalised, and to
\mathbbm{1}_{k}/n
when tensored with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. For
X
a finite type
k
-scheme, we define
\mathscr{N}_{X}:=\pi_{X}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{k}
to be the pullback of
\mathscr{N}_{k}
to
X
. Clearly, this algebra also has the property that when rationalised, it is
\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}
, and that the natural map
\mathbbm{1}_{X}/n\to\mathscr{N}_{X}/n
is an equivalence for
n
a nonzero integer. Furthermore, if
f\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
is the structural map,
??
implies that the natural transformation
f^{*}\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}\to\mathscr{N}_{X}
is an equivalence. We therefore extend the definition of
\mathscr{N}_{X}
to any
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme
f\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
by setting
\mathscr{N}_{X}:=f^{*}\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}
.

Definition 3.1.1
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. The

presentable
\infty
-category of Nori motives over
X
is the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z}):=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{X}}\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

of modules in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
over the algebra
\mathscr{N}_{X}
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. We then define

\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda):=\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z}).

We denote by
\Lambda_{X}
unit object of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)

We can promote this construction into a functor

\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\Lambda}),

and there is a natural transformation
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
:
as
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
is the final object of
\mathrm{Sch}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, the functor
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
actually takes values in
the
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)
-algebras in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
. There is a natural functor

-\otimes(\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}\otimes\Lambda)\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}\otimes\Lambda}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda))

and we can define

\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda):=\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)\otimes_{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)).

On
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
, the operations
f^{*}
,
g_{\sharp}
and
\otimes
for
f
a morphism of schemes and
g
a smooth morphism of schemes are
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)
-linear. Thus, they induce analogous functors for
\mathrm{DN}
: they are the
\mathscr{N}
-linearisation of the functors on
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
. Moreover, if
g
is smooth, the adjunction
(g_{\sharp},g^{*})
is then an internal adjunction to the (
\infty
,2)-category of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)
-linear presentable
\infty
-categories, hence the
\mathscr{N}
-linearisation of each of the two functor actually form an adjunction for
\mathrm{DN}
.
By
[
AGV22
, Theorem 4.6.1 and Remark 4.6.2]
, we then have:

Proposition 3.1.2
.

The functor

\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\Lambda})

is naturally part of a six functors formalism such that the functors of type
f^{*}
,
f_{!}
and
\otimes
are compatible with the forgetful functor to
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. We denote the tensor structure by
\otimes_{\mathrm{N}}
and the internal Hom by
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{N}}
.

Furthermore
\mathrm{DN}
also has some continuity properties when
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
has.

Definition 3.1.3
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme and let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring.
We say that
X
is
\Lambda
-finite if it has finite Krull dimension and

\sup_{x\in X,p\in\Lambda^{\times}}\mathrm{cd}_{p}(\kappa(x))<\infty

where
\mathrm{cd}_{p}(k)
is the Galois cohomological dimension of a field
k
with
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-coefficients.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

Let
X=\lim X_{i}
be a limit of qcqs étale-locally
\Lambda
-finite
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes with affine transition maps.
The natural map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{DN}(X_{i},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)

in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
is an equivalence.

Proof.

The functor sending a pair
(\mathcal{C},A)
consisting of a symmetric monoidal presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
together with a commutative algebra
A
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})
to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
of modules over
A
in
\mathcal{C}
commutes with colimits: this is
[
AGV22
, Lemma 3.5.6]
. The proposition then follows from the same result for étale motives which is
[
EHIK21b
, Lemma 5.1]
.
∎

Proposition 3.1.5
.

The functor
\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
has the same descent properties as
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
. In particular:

1.

It is an étale hypersheaf and a cdh-sheaf.

2.

It is a cdh-hypersheaf over qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite valuative dimension.

3.

It is an h-hypersheaf over Noetherian schemes of finite dimension.

Proof.

Note that the proposition is true if we replace
\mathrm{DN}
by
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. Indeed, since the presentable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
satisfies Nisnevich descent, the localisation property and proper base change, it satisfies cdh-descent as the proof of
[
CD19
, Theorem 3.3.10]
applies in this level of generality; for hyperdescent, this follows from the fact that the cdh topos is hypercomplete over qcqs schemes of finite valuative dimension by
[
EHIK21a
, Corollary 2.4.16]
. Finally, h-descent follows from
[
CD16
, Corollary 5.5.7]
as Bachmann’s Rigidity Theorem
[
BH21
, Theorem 3.1]
allows to remove the hypothesis on the base scheme in
loc. cit.

Now, let
f_{\bullet}\colon X_{\bullet}\to X
be an hyper covering of one of the topologies considered in the proposition. By
[
AC23
, Proposition 2.12]
we have an equivalence of
\infty
-operads

\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda))^{\otimes}\xrightarrow{\sim}\lim_{\Delta}\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X_{i},\Lambda))^{\otimes}

(3.1.A)

where if
\mathcal{C}
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathcal{C})
can be informally described as pairs
(A,M)
with
A
in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})
and
M
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
.
We know that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{0})_{*}\mathscr{N}_{X_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f_{1})_{*}\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}

is a limit diagram in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
. Indeed, as
\mathscr{N}
is pulled back from
X
the above diagram is in fact

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
. Thus, the fiber
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)
over
\mathscr{N}_{X}
of the left-hand side of
??
corresponds to
\lim_{\Delta}\mathrm{DN}(X_{i},\Lambda)
, finishing the proof.
∎

Remark 3.1.6

(Universal property of Nori motives)
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
and let
\Lambda
be a regular ring.
By Drew and Gallauer’s work
[
DG22
]
, we know that
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
is universal among functors

\mathrm{Sch}_{k}^{\mathrm{ft}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\Lambda})

that satisfy
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance,
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stability, smooth base change and étale hyperdescent. We claim that
\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
is universal among the above class of functors
\mathcal{C}
that verify the following additional conditions: the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{liss}}(k)
of dualisable objects in
\mathcal{C}(k)
affords a t-structure and the induced functor
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\Lambda)\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{liss}}(k)
factors the Betti realisation in a way that the functor
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{liss}}(k)\to\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}
is t-exact and conservative. Indeed, for such a
\mathcal{C}
, the universal property of Nori motives over
k
gives us a faithful exact functor
\mathcal{M}(k)\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{liss}}(k)^{\heartsuit}
such that the induced functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k))\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{liss}}(k)\to\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}
is the Betti realisation of Nori motives. In particular, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\Lambda)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DN}(k,\Lambda)}}

which induces, if one denotes by
\mathscr{C}
the commutative algebra representing cohomology with values in
\mathcal{C}(k)
, a map of algebras
\mathscr{N}_{k}\to\mathscr{C}
in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\Lambda)
. By pulling back
\mathscr{C}
over finite type
k
-schemes we obtain morphisms of functors

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda))\to\mathcal{C},

thus the promised morphism of functors

\mathrm{DN}\to\mathcal{C}
.

3.2 Nori motives of geometric origin

The goal of this section is to prove the statements of
[
RT25
]
in the setting of Nori motives. Namely, we define Nori motives of geometric origin and show that they can be characterised by the property of being
constructible
which is more categorical. We also show descent properties as well as the continuity property.

Definition 3.2.1
.

Let
X
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme and let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. The category
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)

of Nori motives of geometric origin
(or simply
geometric Nori motives
) is the thick subcategory of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)
generated by the
f_{\sharp}(\Lambda_{Y})(n)
for
f\colon Y\to X
smooth and
n
an integer.

Theorem 3.2.2
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a morphism of qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension.

1.

The functors of type
f^{*}
,
f_{!}
(the latter for
f
of finite type) and
\otimes_{\mathrm{N}}
preserve geometric objects.

2.

Let
M
and
N
be Nori motives over
X
with
M
geometric and let
P
be a Nori motive over
Y
. Then, the natural transformations below are equivalences:. Then, the natural transformations below are equivalences:

(a)

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}}(M,N)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}}(M,N\otimes\mathbb{Q})

(b)

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{N}}(M,N)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{N}}(M,N\otimes\mathbb{Q})

(c)

f_{*}(P)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to f_{*}(P\otimes\mathbb{Q})

(d)

f^{!}(N)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\to f^{!}(N\otimes\mathbb{Q})
(for
f
of finite type)

3.

Consider a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme
X
of finite dimension that is the limit of a projective system of qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension
(X_{i})
with affine transition maps.
Then, the natural map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X_{i},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)

is an equivalence.

4.

Let
X
be a qcqs
\Lambda
-finite
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, then, a Nori motive
M
is of geometric origin if and only if it is a compact object of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)
.

5.

Assume that the ring
\Lambda
is ind-regular
1
1

1

i.e.
it is a filtered colimit of regular rings.
. A Nori motive
M
over a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension is of geometric origin if and only if it is
constructible
, that is for any open affine
U\subseteq X
, there is a finite stratification
U_{i}\subseteq U
made of constructible locally closed subschemes such that each
M|_{U_{i}}
is dualisable.

Proof.

In the course of this proof, we will denote by
\mathrm{DN}_{c}
the subcategory of constructible Nori motives.

The functors of type
f^{*}
,
f_{\sharp}
,
\otimes_{\mathrm{N}}
and
i_{*}
for
i
a closed immersion readily preserve geometric objects. Using Nagata compactifications, the last remaining part of Assertion 1. is to prove that functors of type
p_{*}
for
p
proper preserve geometric objects which we will prove later.

Now, if
f\colon Y\to X
is smooth and
n
is an integer,
??
implies that
f_{\sharp}(\Lambda_{Y})(n)=\rho_{\mathrm{N}}(f_{\sharp}(\mathbbm{1}_{Y})(n))
. Now, the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
being monoidal, it sends constructible motives to constructible Nori motives. Since geometric motives are constructible using
[
RT25
, Corollary 3.7]
, the Nori motive
f_{\sharp}(\Lambda_{Y})(n)
is constructible. Hence, geometric Nori motives are constructible.

We now prove Assertion 2.(a). By adjunction, we can reduce to the case where
M=\Lambda_{X}
.
But we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}}(\Lambda_{X},N)\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}}(\Lambda_{X},N\otimes\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(\Lambda_{X},\rho^{\mathrm{N}}_{*}N)\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(\Lambda_{X},\rho^{\mathrm{N}}_{*}(N\otimes\mathbb{Q}))}

where the vertical arrows are equivalences by definition of the right adjoint
\rho^{\mathrm{N}}_{*}
to the Nori realisation and the bottom horizontal arrow is an equivalence by
[
RT25
, Proposition 3.1]
together with the fact that
\rho^{\mathrm{N}}_{*}
commutes with colimits as it is a forgetful functor.

Assertions 2.(b) and 2.(c) as well as Assertion 2.(d) for
f
a closed immersion then follow from 2.(a) by exactly the same proof as
[
RT25
, Proposition 3.1]
. Note that Assertion 2.(d) for a closed immersion implies that Assertion 2.(a) holds when
M
is only assumed to be constructible: it allows by dévissage to reduce to the case where
M=\Lambda_{X}
.

We now prove Assertion 4: in the
\Lambda
-finite case, the equivalence between geometric objects and compact objects can be deduced from the same result for
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
which is
[
RT25
, Proposition 3.8]
and
[
Iwa18
, Lemma 2.6]
which states that if a category is
\mathcal{C}
is compactly generated, the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
with
A
some commutative algebra is still compactly generated and that the
A\otimes M
with
M
compact form a set of compact generators. This also implies Assertion 5. in the
\Lambda
-finite case: the fact that constructible objects are compact can then be proved exactly as in
[
RT25
, Proposition 3.8]
.

The next step is to prove the continuity property both for geometric and for constructible Nori motives,
i.e.
that the natural maps

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X_{i},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{DN}_{c}(X_{i},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}_{c}(X,\Lambda)

are equivalences (here
\mathrm{DN}_{c}
denotes constructible Nori motives). This will in particular prove Assertion 3. As geometric motives are constructible, it suffices to prove the full faithfulness for constructible motives which can then be tested after tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
and with
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
for any prime number
p
. In the first case, it is then a consequence of
??
, while in the second case, it is a consequence of Bachmann’s Rigidity Theorem in the form given in
[
RT25
, Theorem 3.2]
and of Bhatt and Matthew’s continuity property for torsion étale sheaves in the form stated in
[
RT25
, Proposition 3.4]
which both apply for derived rings of coefficients. The essential surjectivity of the first functor can then be proved Zariski locally so that we can assume
X
and the
X_{i}
to be affine in which case, the usual spreading-out properties of
[
Gro66
, Théorème 8.10.5, Proposition 17.7.8]
give the result (see the proof of
[
RT25
, Theorem 3.5]
for more details). The essential surjectivity of the second functor is then a consequence of the analogous of
[
RT25
, Lemma 3.9]
which can be proved exactly the same way:

Lemma 3.2.3
.

Let
M
be a constructible Nori motive over a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme
X
of finite dimension. Then, there is a geometric Nori motive
P
as well as a map
P\to M\oplus M[1]
whose cone
C
is such that
C/n\simeq C\oplus C[1]
for some integer
n
.

We can now finish the proof of Assertion 1: we have to prove that the functors of type
p_{*}
for
p
proper preserve geometric objects. This is a consequence of the continuity property for geometric objects and can be proved in the same fashion as in
[
RT25
, Corollary 3.6]
. Now, Assertion 1. implies Assertion 2.(d) for a general
f
of finite which finishes the proof of Assertion 2.

It remains to prove that constructible Nori motives are geometric. As in the case of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, this can be reduced by continuity, localisation and dévissage to the case where
X
is the spectrum of a field and
\Lambda
is regular (see the proof of
[
RT25
, Theorem 4.1]
).

Lemma 3.2.4
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero and let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Then, the Artin motive functor

\iota\colon\mathrm{D}(k_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(k,\Lambda)

is a fully faithful monoidal functor. Furthermore any torsion Nori motive lies in its essential image.

Proof.

If the result is known for
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}
, then the result for an arbitrary ring of coefficients
\Lambda
follows from tensoring both sides with
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}
: this operation preserves full faithfulness according to
[
Hai22
, Lemma 2.14]
.
Now, using
??
,
??
and rigidity of étale motives, it suffices to show that this result holds with
\Lambda=\mathbb{Q}
. Then, the functor
\iota
is the indization of the Artin motive functor

\mathrm{D}(k_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q})^{\omega}\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

which is fully faithful. Indeed we have that the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{Rep}(\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{k}/k),\Lambda))\to\mathrm{D}(k_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q})^{\omega}

is an equivalence, and as all Artin motives are of weight
0
in Nori motives, the abelian category of Artin motives is semi-simple, so that the above functor is fully faithful whenever it is fully faithful on the hearts, which is proven in
[
HMS17
, Proposition 9.4.3]
.
∎

Let
M
be a constructible Nori motive. We have to show that
M
is geometric.
??
yields a geometric Nori motive
P
and a map
P\to M\oplus M[1]
whose cone
C
is such that
C\otimes\mathbb{Q}=0
.

We can then write
C=\iota D
, with
D
a dualisable object of
\mathrm{D}(k_{et},\Lambda)
. In particular, it belongs to the thick stable subcategory generated by the
f_{\sharp}\underline{\Lambda}
for
f
finite étale according to
[
Rui22
, 2.1.2.15]
. Thus, its image
C
through
\iota
is constructible as
\iota
is compatible with the functors
f_{\sharp}
and
f^{*}
for
f
finite étale: indeed
\iota=\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\rho_{!}

with
\rho_{!}
the Artin motive functor to
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
; the same result is true for
\rho_{!}
using
[
CD16
, §4.4.2]
and for
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
by
??
.
Finally,
M\oplus M[1]
is geometric and therefore
M
is geometric.
∎

Corollary 3.2.5
.

Let
\Lambda=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\Lambda_{i}
be a filtered colimit of rings and let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension. Then the canonical map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda_{i})\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As generators of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)
are clearly in the image, it suffices to prove fully faithfulness. Moreover by continuity we can assume that
X
is of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Now this can be proven after tensorisation by
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{Z}/p
. The rational part follows from the fact that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)\otimes\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}
consists of the compact objects of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Q})\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}
and the functor
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Q})\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}\otimes(-)}
commutes with filtered colimits of rings. If we mod out by
p
, then the result follows from
[
RT25
, Lemma 4.3]
.
∎

Corollary 3.2.6
.

Let
\Lambda
be an ind-regular ring. The functor
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
is an étale hypersheaf over qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension.

Proof.

The condition of being constructible is étale local exactly as in
[
RT25
, Lemma 2.5]
.
∎

Corollary 3.2.7
.

Let
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
be a morphism between commutative rings and let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension. The canonical map

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda^{\prime})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We claim that it is sufficient to deal with the case
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}
. Indeed, assume that

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{A}\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)

is an equivalence for all rings
A
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda^{\prime}).

It suffices to prove fully faithfulness as generators of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda^{\prime})
clearly are in the image.
Let
A
be a ring and let
M,N
be objects of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)
. We will use that for any perfect complex of
A
-modules
P
, the canonical map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)}(M,N)\otimes_{A}P\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)}(M,N\otimes_{A}P)

(3.2.A)

is an equivalence: this follows from the fact that
P
is a finite colimit of free
A
-modules. By
??
we can assume that
\Lambda^{\prime}
is a ring of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
, that is a quotient
\mathbb{Z}[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]/I
of a polynomial ring by an ideal. Say we knew the result for
\Lambda^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]
. Then as
\mathbb{Z}[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]/I
is a perfect
\mathbb{Z}[T_{1},\dots,T_{r}]
, the proof would be finished by
??
. By induction it suffices to prove that if the result is known for a Noetherian ring
A
, then we can show that it holds for
A[T]
. But then writing

A[T]=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}A[X]_{\leqslant n}

with
A[X]_{\leqslant n}
the polynomials of degree
\leqslant n
, using
??
we see that is suffices to prove that for
M,N
in
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)
, the map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)}(M,N\otimes_{A}A[X]_{\leqslant n})\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,A)}(M,\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}N\otimes_{A}A[X]_{\leqslant n})

is an equivalence. Finally, this can be checked after tensorisation by
\mathbb{Q}
(where this holds because
M
becomes compact), and after tensorisation by
\mathbb{Z}/p
where this follows from
[
HRS23
, Lemma 5.3]
(note that
loc. cit.
is stated for a ring and not a derived ring, but the proof is the same for our derived ring
A\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p
: pushforwards commutes with uniformly bounded below colimits by
[
BGH20
, Corollary 3.10.5]
and then one has to prove the statement for the internal Hom, which is obvious is
M
is dualisable, and follows in general by dévissage.)
∎

Remark 3.2.8
.

The same proof gives the same result for
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

3.3 Compatibilities of the six functors and Verdier duality

We now study the compatibility of the Nori realisation with the six functors. We also show that the six functors induce functors on constructible motives when restricted to quasi-excellent schemes. Recall that by
[
AGV22
, Theorem 4.6.1]
this is also the case for
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Theorem 3.3.1
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Over the category of qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, the Nori realisation

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}:=-\otimes\mathscr{N}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)

is compatible with the operations

•

f^{*}
,
f_{*}
, for any morphism
f
between qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension.

•

f_{!}
,
f^{!}
, for any morphism
f
of finite type between qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension.

•

\otimes
and
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,-)
for
M
of geometric origin.

Proof.

The case of left adjoints is given by the definitions of the operations of type
f^{*}
,
f_{!}
and
\otimes
on
\mathrm{DN}(-,\Lambda)
. By adjunction, this yields exchange transformations

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}R\to R\rho_{\mathrm{N}}

for
R
of type
f_{*}
,
f^{!}
nd
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,-)
for
M
of geometric origin. The theorem amounts to proving that this exchange transformations are equivalences, which can be tested after tensoring those maps with
\mathbb{Q}
or
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
for any prime number
p
. After tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
, the Nori realisation becomes an equivalence so the exchange maps are also equivalences. After tensoring with
\mathbb{Q}
, those maps can be interpreted as the same maps but with coefficients
\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
since the operations of type
f_{*}
,
f^{!}
and
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,-)
for
M
of geometric origin are compatible with rationalisation by Assertion 2. of
??
. Hence, we can assume that
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra.

Now recall that if
X
is a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, we have

\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)=\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\otimes_{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)}\mathrm{DN}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)

and that the operation
f^{*}
on
\mathrm{DN}
is defined as
f^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{DN}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)}
. Recall that
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)
is compactly generated by
[
EHIK21b
, Lemma 5.1]
and that
\mathrm{DN}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)
is also compactly generated by
[
Iwa18
, Lemma 2.6]
. Recall also that if
f\colon Y\to X
is a morphism of schemes, the functor
f^{*}
preserves compact objects and therefore the functor
f_{*}
is colimit-preserving. We can therefore apply
??
to show that
f_{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{DN}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\Lambda)}
is right adjoint to this functor which exactly means that
f_{*}
is compatible with the Nori realisation. The case of the functor
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,-)
can be deduced by the same argument applied to the functor
M\otimes-
because geometric objects are compact (as the generators are the images of compact étale motives).
∎

Proposition 3.3.2
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. The six functors preserve the category

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
when restricted to quasi-excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension and to morphisms of finite type between these schemes. More precisely

1.

If
X
is a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, then
\otimes_{\mathrm{N}}
induces a monoidal structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)
. It is closed when
X
is quasi-excellent and of finite dimension.

2.

If
f
is a morphism between qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes, then
f^{*}
preserves geometric objects. If
f
is furthermore of finite type, then
f_{!}
also preserves geometric objects.

3.

If
f
is a morphism of finite type between quasi-excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, then
f_{*}
and
f^{!}
preserve geometric objects.

Proof.

The first part of (1), as well as (2) have already been proved in
??
. For the rest of the proposition, we can work over quasi-excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension which are Noetherian by assumption, so that the results of
[
CD16
]
apply.

Let
X
be a quasi-excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension.
The motives of type
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}(M)
with
M
in
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
generate
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)
as a thick subcategory of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)
. Therefore, the result follows from the same result for
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
which is
[
CD16
, Corollary 6.2.14]
and from the compatibility of the six functors with the Nori realisation which is
??
.
∎

Proposition 3.3.3
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Consider an excellent scheme
B
of dimension
2
or less as well as a regular separated
B
-scheme of finite type
S
, endowed with a geometric and
\otimes
-
invertible object
\omega_{S}
in
\mathrm{DN}(S,\Lambda)
. For any separated morphism of finite type
f\colon X\to S
,
we define the
Verdier duality functor

\mathbb{D}_{X}
by the formula

\mathbb{D}_{X}(M):=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{N}}(M,f^{!}(\omega_{S})).

Then, for
any geometric Nori motive
M
, the canonical map
M\to\mathbb{D}_{X}(\mathbb{D}_{X}(M))

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

From the compatibility of the Nori realisation with the six functors (
??
) and the same result for étale motives
[
CD16
, Theorem 6.2.17]
, we deduce the result when
\omega_{S}=\Lambda_{S}
. This implies the result in general using
[
CD19
, Proposition 4.4.22]
.
∎

Remark 3.3.4
.

A priori with rational coefficients we have two Verdier duality functors, at least rationally: the one constructed using the above formula, and the one obtained using the universal property of Nori motives. Thankfully, these two functors are canonically isomorphic by Terenzi’s
[
Ter26
, Proposition 5.19]
. The proof uses the existence of a Nori realisation of rational étale motives.

3.4 The
\ell
-adic realisation

In this paragraph, we define the
\ell
-adic realisation on Nori motives.

Proposition 3.4.1
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring, let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme and let
\ell
be a prime number. The maps

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)_{\mathrm{tors}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{!}}\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)_{\mathrm{tors}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathrm{N}}}\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)_{\mathrm{tors}}

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)^{\wedge}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{(\rho_{!})^{\wedge}_{\ell}}\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)^{\wedge}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{(\rho_{\mathrm{N}})^{\wedge}_{\ell}}\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)^{\wedge}_{\ell}

are equivalences.

Proof.

The second row of equivalences follows from the first and from the identification of
p
-torsion objects with
p
-complete objects. By
??
, it suffices to prove the first identification after tensoring with
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
. The proposition then follows from the rigidity theorem
[
BH21
, Theorem 3.1]
and from the fact that the canonical map
\mathbbm{1}_{X}/n\to\mathscr{N}_{X}/n
is an equivalence.
∎

Definition 3.4.2
.

Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. The big
\ell
-adic realisation of Nori motives is the functor

R_{\ell}^{\mathrm{big}}\colon\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{(-)^{\wedge}_{\ell}}\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}.

Remark 3.4.3
.

As in
[
CD16
, Section 7.2]
, we can endow
\mathrm{D}(-_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
with the six functors formalism and show that the big
\ell
-adic realisation is compatible with the six functors.

We now construct the
\ell
-adic realisation for constructible motives. It will land into the category of constructible sheaves of
[
BS15
,
HRS23
]
.

Construction 3.4.4
.

If
X
is a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, we have maps:

\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{R_{\ell}^{\mathrm{big}}}\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{-\otimes\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}).

The image of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
through the latter is contained in the subcategory of constructible sheaves
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
(that is those complexes whose restriction to any open affine subset are dualisable on a finite stratification).
The change of sites from the étale to the pro-étale site then induces an equivalence

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})

using
[
HRS23
, Proposition 7.1]
. We therefore get a map

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\lim\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}).

[
HRS23
, Proposition 5.1]
ensures that the right-hand side is equivalent to
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
the
\infty
-category of constructible proétale sheaves (again, those complexes whose restriction to any open affine subset are dualisable on a finite stratification but in the pro-étale world). The categories
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
are endowed with the six functors over quasi-excellent base schemes using
[
BS15
, Section 6.7]
; these are defined by passing to the limit from the functors over
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
which are induced from those over
\mathrm{D}(-_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
. This yields a map

R_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

compatible with the six functors over quasi-excellent schemes which we call the
\ell
-adic realisation.

Finally, if
\Lambda
is a localisation of a ring integral and flat over
\mathbb{Z}
, we can tensor the above functor by
\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}
over
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}
to get the
\ell
-adic realisation

R_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda_{\ell}),

where the last equivalence is
[
HS23
, Corollary 2.3]
.

This functor is compatible with the functors of type
\otimes
,
f^{*}
and
f_{!}
and when we restrict to quasi-excellent base schemes and to finite type morphisms, it is compatible with the functors of type
f_{*}
and
f^{!}
. Indeed, the same is true for the
\ell
-adic realisation of étale motives and therefore this follows from
??
.

Proposition 3.4.5
.

Let
\Lambda
be a localisation of a flat integral extension of
\mathbb{Z}
. Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension.

1.

If
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, and
\ell
is a prime number, then the
\ell
-adic realisation functor

R_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda_{\ell})

is conservative for any prime
\ell
.

2.

If
\Lambda
is not a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, the family of the
R_{\ell}
where
\ell\notin\Lambda^{\times}
is conservative. This means that if
M
is an object
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)
such that for all
\ell\not\in\Lambda^{\times}
, the object
R_{\ell}(M)
vanishes, then
M\simeq 0
.

Proof.

We first show the first assertion.
By continuity (
??
), we can reduce to the case where
X
is a finite type
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. As
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, we have by
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 4.19]
an equivalence

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\Lambda}.

Moreover using the description of mapping spectra in tensor products provided by
[
HL23
, Proposition 3.5.5]
we see that if the functor

R_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})

is conservative, so is the functor of the proposition. Thus this follows from
[
IM24
, Proposition 6.11]
.

We now prove the second assertion. As the family
\{-\otimes\mathbb{Q}
,
-\otimes\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\mid\ell\notin\Lambda^{\times}\}
is conservative, by the first point, it suffices to show that if
R_{\ell}(M/\ell)
vanishes, then so does
M/\ell
for
M
a geometric motive. This amounts to the rigidity theorem.
∎

We finish by a conjectural aspect of our construction: let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension and let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. By construction we have a functor

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda).

Proposition 3.4.6
.

Assume that for all fields
K
of finite transcendence degree over
\mathbb{Q}
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(K,\mathbb{Q})
is endowed with a motivic t-structure (see the discussion around
??
). Then for
X
as above, the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

First, note that the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
is the tensorisation by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}
of the functor with coefficients
\mathbb{Z}
, so that it suffices to deal with this case. Moreover as this functor is an equivalence after being tensored by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, it suffices to check that it becomes an equivalence after being tensored by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. As
\mathscr{N}_{X}
is pulled back from
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
, it also suffices to prove that
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
is an equivalence over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})
. The result then follows from
[
Tub25b
, Theorem 6.22]
.
∎

Remark 3.4.7
.

The hypothesis in the previous proposition could be weakened. Indeed it is very possible that using Pridham’s
[
Pri20
, Section 2.5]
, one can prove that if
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})
has a motivic t-structure, then the functor

\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q}))

is fully faithful, so that, because its image is
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q}))
(because
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q})
is Noetherian, see
[
Hub93
]
), this induces an equivalence
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Q}))
, thus the functor

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DN}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})

is an equivalence, meaning that the map of rings
\mathbbm{1}_{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\mathscr{N}_{k,\mathbb{Q}}
is an equivalence. In other terms, to prove that
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
has a motivic t-structure for all
X
as above and
\Lambda
, it “suffices” to prove that
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})
has a motivic t-structure.

Another aspect of the construction is that it gives evidence for a conjecture of Ayoub (
[
Ayo17b
, Conjecture 2.8]
), up to the above
??
:

Proposition 3.4.8
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
.
Denote by
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}
the Verdier quotient of
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})
by the kernel of the Betti realisation.
Then the presentable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}
admits a separated t-structure whose heart is
canonically equivalent to
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})
, and the natural functor

\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is Noetherian, we can apply
[
Kra05
, Proposition 3.6]
to its indisation. This implies that the canonical functor

J\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))

is a Bousfield localisation (note that what is called
\mathrm{K}(\mathrm{Inj}(-))
in
loc. cit.
is nothing but
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}((-)^{\omega})
by
[
Kra05
, Proposition 2.3]
).

We now only have to show that
J
and the Betti realisation have the same kernel. This amounts to the conservativity of the functor

R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}

obtained by deriving the Betti realisation. Take an object
M
to be killed by
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{B}}
. As the t-structure on
\mathrm{D}(\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
is separated, we can assume
M
to be in the heart. By
[
Hub93
, Lemma 1.3]
we can write
M=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}M_{i}
as a filtered colimit of objects
M_{i}
of
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})
with injective transition maps. In particular, because
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{B}}(M)
vanishes, so does each
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{B}}(M_{i})
and therefore
M_{i}
by conservativity of the Betti realisation on Nori motives.
∎

Remark 3.4.9
.

Using this, we can show that a conjecture of Ayoub (
[
Ayo17b
, Conjecture 2.8]
) holds for Nori motives:
Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. Let
M\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
be a complex of Nori motives and let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})
be killed by the Betti realisation. Then the group

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{F},M)=0

vanishes.

Indeed, because
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q})
is Noetherian the above
??
and
[
Hub93
, Proposition 2.2]
imply that the functor

\pi\colon\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}

is fully faithful when restricted to
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
.
We can write
\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}N_{i}
as a filtered colimit of objects in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Q}))
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(\mathcal{F},M)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{i}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})}(N_{i},M)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{i}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}}(\pi(N_{i}),\pi(M))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}}(\pi(\mathcal{F}),\pi(M))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}(k,\mathbb{Q})^{\flat}}(0,\pi(M))=0.

4 The abelian category of ordinary Nori motives.

4.1 Relative Nori motives as a pullback

Until further notice,
k
is an algebraically closed subfield of
\mathbb{C}
. For each finite type
k
-scheme
X
, Ayoub constructed in
[
Ayo10
]
a Betti realisation functor

\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})

to the derived category of analytic sheaves of abelian groups on
X^{\mathrm{an}}
. Let
\mathscr{B}_{k}
be the algebra in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
representing Betti cohomology
[
Ayo22
, Notation 1.57]
and if
X
is a finite type
k
-scheme, let
\mathscr{B}_{X}
be the pullback of
\mathscr{B}_{k}
to
X
. Ayoub considered the functor on finite type
k
-schemes

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{B}_{(-)}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z}))\colon\left(\mathrm{Sch}_{k}^{ft}\right)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}})

of which he gave an explicit description in
[
Ayo22
, Theorem 1.93]
: he proved that the tautological functor

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{B}_{(-)}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z}))
factors the refined Betti realisation

\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})

where
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
denotes the category of constructible analytic complexes, and that for any finite type
k
-scheme
X
, the induced functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{B}_{X}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
is fully faithful. Its essential image is furthermore compactly generated: it is the indisation of a full subcategory
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
of complexes of
geometric origin
.

The realisation to Nori motives

\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})

factors the refined Betti realisation of étale motives, hence there exists a map of algebra

\varphi_{X}\colon\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathscr{B}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}

compatible with pullback by finite type morphisms.

Proposition 4.1.1
.

Let
X
be a
k
-scheme of finite-type.
There exist a natural map of algebras
\mathscr{N}_{X}\to\mathscr{B}_{X}
giving back
