where we recognise the Hasse fracture square of
\mathscr{N}_{\mathbb{Z}}
(the arrow are the correct ones because the equivalence
\mathscr{N}_{X}/n\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{B}_{X}/n
factors the equivalence
\mathbbm{1}_{X}/n\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{B}_{X}/n
.)
∎

Therefore we have a canonical functor

\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z}).

Corollary 4.1.2
.

Relative Nori motives with integral coefficients (even relative to a non algebraically closed field) have a Betti realisation

\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z}).

which is compatible with the six functors.

Proposition 4.1.3
.

The square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})}

is cartesian in
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Sch}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}}))
.

Proof.

It suffices to check the proposition after evaluating at a finite type
k
-scheme
X
. If we let
P(X)
be the pullback of the proposition, there is a functor
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to P(X)
. This functor preserves compact objects (note that
k
is algebraically closed thus
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})\simeq\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
by
??
), thus its right adjoint commutes with colimits, whence commutes with
-\otimes\mathbb{Q}
so that we may use
??
.
∎

Corollary 4.1.4
.

Let
k
be any subfield of
\mathbb{C}
and let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme.
The Betti realisation

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})

is conservative.

Proof.

Indeed, the functor
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X\times_{k}\overline{k},\mathbb{Z})
is conservative by continuity and Galois descent (if
M_{\overline{k}}=0
, there exists a finite extension
L
of
k
such that
M_{L}
is zero, and then by Galois descent the functor
(-)_{L}
is conservative, thus
M=0
).
Then by
??
the functor
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X_{\overline{k}},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
is conservative: if
R_{\mathrm{B}}(M)=0
, then by conservativity of the realisation rationally we have that
M\otimes\mathbb{Q}=0
, thus both
R_{\mathrm{B}}(M)
and
M\otimes\mathbb{Q}
are zero thus
M=0
in the pullback.
∎

The pullback square of
??
allows us to endow the
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
with a t-structure. Recall first that if
\Lambda
is regular, the category
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
is endowed with a t-structure induced by the t-structure of
\mathrm{D}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
. Lurie’s
[
Lur
, Lemma C.2.4.3]
ensures that
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
is endowed with a t-structure whose negative (resp. positive) objects are the filtered colimits of negative (resp. positive) objects, and that therefore, the canonical functor

\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)

is t-exact. One consequence of
[
Ayo22
, Theorem 1.93]
, is that
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
has a t-structure induced by that of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
; we will denote its heart by
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
. Using
[
Lur
, Lemma C.2.4.3]
again, the stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
has a t-structure which we call the
ordinary t-structure
.

On the other hand, we have
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Q})=\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))
. In
[
Tub25b
, Definition 4.1]
, the second author defined a t-structure on
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))
that is called the
constructible t-structure
in the quoted paper but that we will call the
ordinary t-structure
in this paper for consistency. Denote its heart by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, then by
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 4.19]
, the natural map

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))

is an equivalence. Furthermore, the functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})
is t-exact with the ordinary t-structure on both sides. Therefore, using
??
, we get

Proposition 4.1.5
.

Assume that
k
is an algebraically closed subfield of
\mathbb{C}
.
Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme, there is a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
such that the rationalisation, endowed with its ordinary t-structure, and the Betti realisation are t-exact. Furthermore, we have a pullback square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q}).}

Remark 4.1.6
.

Using the ideas of this section, we can define a category of Nori motivic spectra which is akin to the category
\mathrm{SH}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)
of étale motivic spectra when
X
is a scheme of finite type over an
algebraically closed
field of characteristic
0
. First, let
\mathscr{B}_{X}^{\mathbb{S}}
be the algebra in
\mathrm{SH}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)
representing Betti cohomology with
\mathbb{S}
-coefficients. Once again, Ayoub constructed in
[
Ayo22
, Theorem 1.93]
an equivalence
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{B}_{X}^{\mathbb{S}}}(\mathrm{SH}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X))\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{S})
, where
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{S})
is the full subcategory of the category
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{S})
of constructible analytic spectral sheaves, made of objects of geometric origin and is endowed with an induced t-structure.

By analogy with
??
, we can define
\mathscr{N}^{\mathbb{S}}_{X}
to fit in the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}_{X}^{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

and then define
\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{Nori}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X):={\mathrm{Mod}}_{\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\mathbb{S}}}(\mathrm{SH}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X))
. We now claim that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{Nori}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})}

is cartesian. The full faithfulness of the map
\Psi_{X}\colon\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{Nori}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)\to P(X)
(where
P(X)
denotes the pullback) follows from the general claim that if
A=B\times_{D}C
is a pullback of commutative algebra objects in a stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, the map
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{B}}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{D}(\mathcal{C})}\mathrm{Mod}_{C}(\mathcal{C})
is fully faithful which can be seen using a direct computation of mapping spectra. To show that the map
\Psi_{X}
is essentially surjective, note that
P(X)
is endowed with an ordinary t-structure by
??
so that it suffices to reach the objects of its heart. We claim that it is the case. Indeed the functor

-\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{S})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})

yields the equivalence
\tau^{\geqslant 0}(-\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z})
between the hearts, therefore we have an equivalence
P(X)^{\heartsuit}\to\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\heartsuit}
.
Using that at the generic point
\eta
for a projective smooth morphism
f
, the object
f_{*}\mathbb{Z}
is the sum in
\mathrm{DN}(\eta,\mathbb{Z})
of its cohomology objects (by Deligne’s criterion for degeneracy of spectral sequences
[
Del68
, Théorème 1.5]
), one can prove the claim at the generic point, and then localisation enables one to prove that
\Psi_{X}
is an equivalence in general.
Hence we have an ordinary motivic t-structure on
\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{Nori}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)
whose heart is the same as that of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

4.2 The ordinary t-structure on geometric Nori motives

Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme of finite dimension. In this section we construct an ordinary t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. Note that by
??
we already have a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(k,\mathbb{Z})
for any subfield
k
of
\mathbb{C}
.
By continuity and because a filtered colimit of stable
\infty
-categories with
t
-structures and t-exact functors has a natural t-structure, we can assume that
X
is a finite type
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. In this case we may use
the punctual gluing of t-structures due to Vaish
[
Vai20
, Proposition 3.8]
.
In order to use his result, we have to verify the condition he calls "continuity of the t-structure", which is the purpose of the next proposition. Let us first recall how the t-structure is defined:

Definition 4.2.1
.

For each
x\in X
, denote by
i_{x}\colon\operatorname{Spec}(\kappa(x))\to X
the inclusion of
x
in
X
.
An object
M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is called

1.

punctually connective
if for each
x\in X
we have
i_{x}^{*}M\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\kappa(x),\mathbb{Z}))^{\leqslant 0}

2.

punctually coconnective
if for each
x\in X
we have
i_{x}^{!}M\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\kappa(x),\mathbb{Z}))^{\geqslant 1}
.

We denote by
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0}
and
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 1}
the associated full subcategories.

Proposition 4.2.2
.

Let
X
be a finite type
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. Let
x\in X
and let
M_{x}\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\kappa(x),\mathbb{Z}))
.

1.

If
M\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\kappa(x),\mathbb{Z}))^{\leqslant 0}
there exists a nonempty open subset
U
of
\overline{\{x\}}
and an object
M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(U,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0}
such that
i_{x}^{*}M=M_{x}
.

2.

If
M\in\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}(\kappa(x),\mathbb{Z}))^{\geqslant 1}
there exists a nonempty open subset
U
of
\overline{\{x\}}
and an object
M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(U,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 1}
such that
i_{x}^{!}M=M_{x}
.

Proof.

In both cases we can assume that
x
is a generic point of
X
and that
X
is irreducible, and even smooth up to replace
X
by one of its dense open subsets. In this case both
i_{x}^{*}
and
i_{x}^{!}
agree. Thus the proof of the two cases are almost the same and we only deal with the first point.
By continuity, we know that there exist some
U_{0}\subset X
nonempty and open, and
M_{0}\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(U_{0},\mathbb{Z})
such that
i_{x}^{*}M_{0}=M_{x}
. Now the Betti realisation being conservative, we know that there exists some integer

C\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that if
p
is a prime verifying that the multiplication by
p
is zero on
M_{0}
, then
p\mid C
. Moreover, there is some other integer
a>0
such that the Betti realisation of
M_{0}
lives in degrees
[-a,a]
.
Because
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}
and the functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
are conservative and t-exact, we see that for each prime number
\ell
, the
\ell
-adic realisation of
M_{0}
is concentrated in degrees
[-a,a]
. Let
U_{1}
be a nonempty open subset of
U_{0}
on which
M_{0}
is dualisable.
For each
j\in[1,a]
and each prime number
\ell
dividing
C
, because
i_{x}^{*}\mathrm{H}^{j}(R_{\ell}(M_{1}))
is zero and
\mathrm{H}^{j}(R_{\ell}(M_{1}))
is a local system on
(U_{1})_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, there exists a nonempty open subset
U_{j,\ell}
of
U_{1}
such that the restriction to
U_{j,\ell}
of
\mathrm{H}^{j}(R_{\ell}(M_{1}))
is zero.
We let
U
be the intersection of all of those
U_{j,\ell}
and
M
be the restriction of
M_{1}
to
U
.
Then we see that
R_{\mathrm{B}}(M)
is connective, thus
M
is punctually connective, as wanted.
∎

Theorem 4.2.3
.

The conditions of
??
define a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and the Betti realisation is t-exact. If
X
is of finite type over an algebraically closed field embedded in
\mathbb{C}
, this t-structure is the same as the t-structure constructed in
??
. Moreover, for any qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme
X
of finite dimension this induces a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
such that all
\ell
-adic realisations are t-exact.

Proof.

The t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is given by the above proposition together with
[

, Proposition 3.8]
. The fact that the Betti realisation is t-exact can be checked on stalked at closed points, where it is true. Conservativity of the Betti realisation
??
then implies the comparison with the t-structure of
??
. Thus the
\ell
-adic realisation are also t-exact, and when we endow
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, for
X
a general finite dimentional qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme by continuity using
??
, the

-adic realisation stay t-exact because if
X=\lim X_{i}
is a cofiltered limit with affine transitions, although the functor

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{i,\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

is not an equivalence, it is t-exact.
∎

Definition 4.2.4
.

Let
X
be a finite dimentional qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. The category of
ordinary Nori motives

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is the heart of the t-structure given by
??
. We call the latter the
ordinary t-structure
.

Remark 4.2.5
.

All the construction above would work if we replace
\mathbb{Z}
by a localisation of the ring of integers of a number field, finite or not over
\mathbb{Q}
. This is because in this case the category
\mathcal{M}(k,\Lambda)
is well-behaved (because
\Lambda
is a Dedekind ring), so that
??
and
??
work in that case. For example, we have a t-structure with
\overline{\mathbb{Z}}
-coefficients.

Remark 4.2.6
.

As the
\ell
-adic realisation is also conservative by
??
, any t-exactness property of the ordinary t-structure on
\ell
-adic sheaves transfers to the ordinary t-structure on Nori motives.

Remark 4.2.7
.

Let
k
be a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
and let
X
be a
k
-variety.
Arapura considered in
[
Ara13
]
an abelian category of motivic sheaves modelled on constructible sheaves. By construction, his category

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Arapura}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
has an universal property, so that there exists a faithful exact functor
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Arapura}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
compatible with the Betti realisations. We do not know much about this functor, except that it is an equivalence for
X=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
.

4.3 The derived category of ordinary Nori motives

We now prove that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}
is the derived category of ordinary Nori motives following Nori
[
Nor02
]
. To that end, we first need:

Lemma 4.3.1
.

The functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(-,\Lambda))
is a finitary étale hypersheaf on qcqs schemes of finite dimension.

Proof.

The finitarity of the functor follows from
??
and the continuity of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}
. Because for
f
an étale map, the functors
f^{*}
and
f_{!}
are t-exact, they exist on
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(-,\Lambda))
as an adjunction, compatible with the realisation functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(-,\Lambda))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)

which is conservative. Thus, because
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
is an étale hypersheaf, the functor
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(-,\Lambda)
is an étale sheaf: it is a sub presheaf of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
and the cohomological amplitude with respect to the ordinary t-structure can be tested étale-locally. We can now apply the same proof as
[
Tub25b
, Theorem 5.24]
to conclude.
∎

Theorem 4.3.2
.

Let
\Lambda
be a regular ring which is flat over
\mathbb{Z}
. Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension. Then, the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By
??
, continuity and Zariski hyperdescent for
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\Lambda)
, we can assume that
X
is of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
and separated.
Note that by
[
Bei87
, Lemma 1.4]
, to prove the theorem, it is sufficient to prove that for any pair of Nori motives
M,N
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)
and any integer
i>0
, the map

\mathrm{Ext}^{i}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,N)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda))}(M,N[i])\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,N[i])

is an isomorphism, which, given the characterisation of the
\mathrm{Ext}^{i}
, is equivalent to the functor

\mathrm{E}_{M}^{i}:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)}(M,(-)[i])\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{Ab}

being effaceable for each
i>0
and each
M
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)
. More explicitly, this means that for every Nori motive
N
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)
, there exists a monomorphism
f\colon N\to P
whose image under our functor
\mathrm{E}^{i}_{M}
vanishes.

The proof of this fact is then very similar to the proof the second author gave, following Nori, in the case of Nori motives with rational coefficients (see
[
Nor02
,
Tub25b
]
). Until the end of this proof, we will call
admissible
an Nori motive
M
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)
such that for all
i>0
the functor
\mathrm{E}_{M}^{i}
is effaceable.

By remarking that all the proofs that lead to
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 4.16]
work verbatim with integral coefficients (because we only use the four operations
f^{*},f_{*},f^{!},f_{!}
, geometric arguments and the vanishing
[
Tub25b
, Lemma 2.6]
works with integral coefficients because Nori proved it in this generality in
[
Nor02
, Proposition 2.2]
), we know that the constant object
\Lambda_{X}
is admissible on
X
. To be more precise, we first prove by induction on
n
the admissibility of the constant object on
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbb{Q}}
(the case
n=0
is true because we already know the theorem in that case by
??
, while the induction uses well-chosen projection to
\mathbb{A}^{n-1}
and the already cited
[
Tub25b
, Lemma 4.6]
). Then, using Noether normalisation one can prove that the result is true for any affine scheme. Finally, because our scheme is separated, we can do an induction on the numbers of affine open subsets needed to cover
X
and deduce that
\Lambda_{X}
is indeed admissible on
X

The next step is to prove that any torsion-free lisse sheaf
L
on
X
is admissible. This follows from the fact that because
L
is dualisable, the functor
-\otimes L
is t-exact because
L
is torsion-free (this can be checked after realisation and then on stalks so that this reduces to the same statement about modules) and left adjoint of a t-exact functor; thus by
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 4.13]
the object
\Lambda\otimes L=L
is admissible. Using
[
Tub25b
, Corollary 4.13]
again, the functor
f_{!}
for
f
étale preserves admissibility: the functors
f^{*}
and
f_{!}
are t-exact because they are t-exact after taking the Betti realisation. Moreover by
[
Tub25b
, Lemma 4.10]
, if one has a short exact sequence

0\to M^{\prime}\to M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0

with
M^{\prime}
and
M^{\prime\prime}
admissible, then
M
is admissible. Now if
L
is a lisse motive, then we have the short exact sequence

0\to L_{\mathrm{tors}}\to L\to L_{\mathrm{fr}}\to 0

with
L_{\mathrm{tors}}
the biggest torsion subobject and
L_{\mathrm{fr}}
torsion-free. Note that
L_{\mathrm{tors}}
and
L_{\mathrm{fr}}
are also lisse because they are after taking the Betti realisation. Hence
L_{\mathrm{fr}}
is admissible.

We claim that
L_{\mathrm{tors}}
is also admissible. As it is torsion, it is in fact of the form
\iota(F)
for a lisse étale sheaf on
X
: it is of the form
\iota(F)
because of
??
and
F
is lisse because the functor
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
is conservative and the image of
F
in
\mathrm{DN}(X,\Lambda)
is dualisable, and thus its image in
\mathrm{D}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\Lambda)
is also dualisable which yields the dualisability of
F
by conservativity. Choose some
f\colon U\to X
étale such that
f^{*}F=N
is a constant
\Lambda
-module. We can choose a surjection
\Lambda^{n}\to N
, so that by t-exactness of
f_{!}
, we have a composition of surjections
f_{!}\Lambda^{n}\twoheadrightarrow f_{!}f^{*}F\twoheadrightarrow F
. Let
K
be the kernel of this composed surjection. It is also a lisse and torsion-free étale sheaf: the ring
\Lambda
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}
which is a Dedeking domain, so that a subobject of a torsion-free object is again torsion-free. Thus, in
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda))
we have an exact triangle

\iota(K)\to\iota(f_{!}\Lambda^{n})\to L_{\mathrm{tors}}.

Because
\iota(K)
and
\iota(f_{!}\Lambda^{n})
are admissible, for any motive
P\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\Lambda)
the maps

\mathrm{map}_{D^{b}(\mathcal{M}(X,\Lambda))}(\iota(K),P)\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)}(\iota(K),P)

and

\mathrm{map}_{D^{b}(\mathcal{M}(X,\Lambda))}(\iota(f_{!}\Lambda^{n}),P)\to\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\Lambda)}(\iota(f_{!}\Lambda^{n}),P)

are equivalences. Thus the analogous map for
L_{\mathrm{tors}}
is an equivalence so that
L_{\mathrm{tors}}
is also admissible. This implies that
L
is admissible. Now, the same proof as
[
Tub25b
, Theorem 4.18]
(which is the same proof as
[
Nor02
, Proposition 3.10]
) gives by dévissage that any motive is admissible, finishing the proof.
∎

4.4 Applications: motivic Leray spectral sequence; arc descent

The existence of ordinary integral Nori motives implies that the Leray spectral sequence, with integral coefficients, is motivic; this is a generalisation of
[
IM24
, Section 5.4]
and
[
Ara05
]
.

Proposition 4.4.1
.

Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme with
k
a subfield of
\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be an object of the abelian category
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
of constructible sheaves of abelian groups over
X
.

Assume that
\mathcal{F}
is motivic, meaning that it is the image of some integral Nori motive under the Betti realisation. Let

X\xrightarrow{f}Y\xrightarrow{g}Z

be morphisms of finite type
\mathbb{C}
-schemes. Then the Leray spectral sequence

\mathrm{E}_{2}^{p,q}=\mathrm{R}^{p}g_{*}\mathrm{R}^{q}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\Rightarrow\mathrm{R}^{p+q}(g\circ f)_{*}\mathcal{F}

is the image of a spectral sequence in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Z)
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of the existence of the ordinary t-structure and the functoriality of pushforwards. Indeed, let
M\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(X)
and consider the filtered object

\dots g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i+1}f_{*}M\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i}f_{*}M\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i-1}f_{*}M\to\dots

of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Z})
(the truncation functors are for the ordinary t-structure). By
[
Lur22
, Proposition 1.2.2.14]
(applied in
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Z})
so that the category considered has all sequential colimits and a t-structure compatible with sequential colimits by
[
Lur
, Lemma C.2.4.3]
), we obtain a converging spectral sequence in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}(Z)
whose first page reads

E_{1}^{p,q}=\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(\mathrm{cofib}(g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant p+1}f_{*}M\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant p}f_{*}M))

and converges to
\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i}f_{*}M)
. Using that the extension of
g_{*}
to
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{Z})
commutes with colimits by definition, and the fact that
f_{*}M
is bounded, we can rewrite the abutement of the spectral sequence as
\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(g_{*}f_{*}M)
.
The triangle for the truncation functors gives that
\mathrm{cofib}(g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant p+1}f_{*}M\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant p}f_{*}M)\simeq g_{*}\mathrm{H}^{p}(f_{*}M)[-p]
. Thus, reindexing
E_{1}
to
E_{2}
to fit the general convention, we obtain the motivic Leray spectral sequence

E_{2}^{p,q}=\mathrm{H}^{q}g_{*}\mathrm{H}^{p}f_{*}M\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(g\circ f)_{*}M.

Applying the Betti realisation to the filtered object

\dots g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i+1}f_{*}M\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i}f_{*}M\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i-1}f_{*}M\to\dots

and letting
\mathcal{F}
be the Betti realisation of
M
yields a filtered object

\dots g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i+1}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\to g_{*}\tau^{\leqslant i-1}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\to\dots

by t-exactness of the Betti realisation (see
??
) and its compatibility with pushforwards (
??
). The usual Leray spectral sequence is by definition obtained from this filtered object using the same tools as above. Unwinding the definitions, this proves that it is the image of the motivic Leray spectral sequence through the Betti realisation.
∎

As an application of the existence of the ordinary t-structure, we prove that Nori motives afford arc-descent.

Theorem 4.4.2
.

The functor

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})

satisfies arc-hyperdescent over qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension. This means that for any arc-hypercovering
X_{\bullet}\to X
(
[
BM21
, Definition 1.2]
) where
X
and each
X_{n}
are
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, the functor

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X_{n},\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

As

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})=\bigcup_{n}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}

we see that to prove that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
is an arc-hypersheaf, it suffices to show that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}
is an arc-hypersheaf (hence even an arc-sheaf), where the truncation is taken for the ordinary t-structure.

The presheaf
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}
takes values in
\mathrm{Cat}_{2n+1}
which is compactly generated by cotruncated objects (see
[
BM21
, Example 3.6 (3)]
). We consider

\mathcal{F}\colon(\mathrm{Sch}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{2n+1}

its left Kan extension to
(\mathrm{Sch}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathrm{qcqs}})^{\mathrm{op}}
the category of (non necessarily finite-dimensional) qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes. By
??
we have that
\mathcal{F}(X)=\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}
for
X
of finite dimension. We may now apply the main theorem of
[
BM21
]
: a finitary
v
-sheaf taking values in an
\infty
-category which is compactly generated by cotruncated objects is an arc-sheaf if and only if it is aic-
v
-excisive. By definition,
\mathcal{F}
is finitary.

First, we want to prove that
\mathcal{F}
is a
v
-sheaf. Given a
v
-covering
f\colon Y\to X
, we can write it as
\lim_{i}(f_{i}\colon Y_{i}\to X_{i})
with
X_{i}
and
Y_{i}
of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
. Moreover, as the sheaf condition for
f
and
\mathcal{F}
is finite because it is a totalisation in an
\infty
-category which is compactly generated by cotruncated objects, it is compatible with the filtered colimit of the continuity. Hence, it suffices to show that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}
is a
v
-sheaf when restricted to finite type
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes, but for Noetherian schemes the
v
-topology and the
h
-topology agree, so that this is follows from
??
and
??
2
2

2

In fact in our setting there is a simpler proof thanks to the t-structure: one can do as in
[
BM21
, Proposition 5.11]
, in which they reduce
v
-descent to descent along proper surjective maps thanks to étale hyperdescent, and then the proper base change theorem implies the result, with a combination of Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem and the finitary property. This proof essentially dates back to Deligne in
[
Del90
]
.
.

Next, we prove that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies aic-
v
-excision. Recall that this means that absolulety integrally closed valuation ring
V
which is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra, together with a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subset V
, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{Spec}(V))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{Spec}(V/\mathfrak{p}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{Spec}(V_{\mathfrak{p}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}(\operatorname{Spec}(\kappa(\mathfrak{p})))}}

is cartesian. Let
(V,\mathfrak{p})
be such datum. By
[
BM21
, Lemma 2.22]
, we can write
V=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}V_{i}
as a filtered colimits of absolulety integrally closed valuation subrings
V_{i}
such that each

has finite rank (this is equivalent to being finite-dimensional by
[

, Lemma 2.3.1]
) and each map
V_{i}\to V_{j}
is faithfully flat. As filtered colimits commute with finite limits and
\mathcal{F}
is finitary, we may assume that
V
is finite-dimensional (by replacing

by

and
\mathfrak{p}
by
\mathfrak{p}\cap V_{i}
). In the setting of
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, we can prove more generally Minor excision: assume that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

is a Milnor square (
[
BM21
, Definition 1.11]
) of qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension, we claim that applying
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}
to this square yields a cartesian square. We first prove it for
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
. Firstly, the functor

F\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Z})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{Z})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{Z})

is fully faithful. This can be checked after tensoring the mapping spectra with
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbb{Z}/p
for all primes
p
. The mod
p

[

, Theorem 3.1]
) and
[

, Theorem 5.13]
. Rationally, the
-\otimes\mathbb{Q}
goes inside the mapping spectra thanks to

and we can use

\mathrm{DN}(-,\mathbb{Q})=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{N}_{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))
satisfies Milnor excision because

satisfies it by

EHIK21b
, Theorem 5.6]
. Now, the compact objects of a finite limit of compactly generated

-categories are the finite limit of the compact objects, thus we even have that

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Q})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{Q})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{Q}).

Secondly, we prove essential surjectivity. As we already proved full faithfullness, we are allowed to use dévissage and retracts of objects of the essential image also are in the essential image.
We first claim that the rationalisation of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Z})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{Z})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{Z})
is

. Indeed there is a fully faithful functor

(\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Z})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{Z})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{Z}))\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{Q})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{Q})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{Q}),

as the rationalisation is the idempotent completion of the
\infty
-category where we replace all mapping spectra by their rationalisation. This functor is essentially surjective because the target is idempotent complete. In particular, using
[
BG23
, Recolletion 2.13]
, we see that given

, there exists
M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X)
and a map
N\oplus N[1]\to F(M)
which is an equivalence after rationalisation. In particular, the cofiber
C
of this map is a (fiber product of) geometric motives that satisfy

. By
??
and the fact that we are looking at a finite limit, this implies that there is some
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that the multiplication by
n
on

is the zero map. In particular, we have that
C/n\simeq C\oplus C[1]
, and it suffices to show that
C/n
is in the image of
F
. By doing a dévissage, we may assume that

is a prime number. In that case, we have that
C/p
comes from

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{F}_{p})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{c}(Z,\mathbb{F}_{p})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{c}(W,\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathrm{DN}_{c}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p}),

by
??
. Using rigidity
[
BH21
, Theorem 3.1]
and Milnor excision for constructible étale sheaves
[

, Theorem 5.13]
, this object
C/p
comes from
\mathrm{DN}_{c}(X,\mathbb{F}_{p})\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{F}_{p})
. This means that we have some

such that the image of

in
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Z,\mathbb{F}_{p})\times_{\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(W,\mathbb{F}_{p})}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})
is

. Because
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{F}_{p})
is generated as a thick subcategory of itself by

with

, this implies that

, which is the image of
F
as the latter is fully faithful. This proves that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
satisfies Milnor excision for this square of finite dimension
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes.

To prove that
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(-,\mathbb{Z})^{[-n,n]}
satisfies excision as well, it suffices to show that if

belongs to
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and is such that

and

are in cohomological degrees
