then in fact
M
is also in degrees
[-n,n]
. This is true because the map
Z\sqcup Y\to X
is surjective hence the hypothesis imply that for any point
x
of
X
, the motive
x^{*}M
in
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(x,\mathbb{Z})
is concentrated in degrees
[-n,n]
, so that
M
is also concentrated in degrees
[-n,n]
.
∎

Remark 4.4.3
.

Conjecturally, the category
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
has an ordinary t-structure after rationalisation. In that case the functor
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is an equivalence because it is an equivalence rationalised and mod
n
for any nonzero integer
n
, so that
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
is a finitary arc-hypersheaf and the category
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
has an ordinary t-structure without needing to rationalise. In fact, it would suffice to prove that
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Q})
has a motivic t-structure (see
??
).

5 Perverse Nori motives.

5.1 The perverse t-structure

In this subsection, we define the perverse t-structure on Nori motives. The definition is very similar to that of the perverse t-structure on constructible complexes of
\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-modules given in
[
BBD82
, Section 2.2]
. We also prove that the derived category of perverse Nori motives is
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}
, which is in the spirit of
[
Bei87
]
.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

Let
X
be an excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension endowed with a dimension function
\delta
3
3

3

See
[
ILO14
, Exposé XIV, Définition 2.1.8]
for the definition of a dimension function.
.
We let

{}^{p}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0}:=\{M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\mid\forall x\in X,i_{x}^{*}(M)\leqslant-\delta(x)\},

{}^{p}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 0}:=\{M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\mid\forall x\in X,i_{x}^{!}(M)\geqslant-\delta(x)\}.

Then, the pair
({}^{p}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0},{}^{p}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 0})
defines a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
such that the
\ell
-adic realisation functor

R_{\ell}\colon\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

is t-exact when the right-hand side is endowed with its perverse t-structure.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
[
BBD82
, Section 2.2]
but we include a sketch for the reader’s convenience.
Consider couples
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})
where
\mathcal{S}
is a finite stratification of
X
by locally closed connected regular subschemes, and
\mathcal{L}
is the data, for each stratum
Z
of
\mathcal{S}
of a finite set
\mathcal{L}(Z)
of dualisable Nori motives on
Z
. We denote by
\mathrm{DN}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})}(X,\mathbb{Z})
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
made of
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})
-constructible Nori motives, that is the motives
M
such that, for each stratum
Z
of
\mathcal{S}
and each integer
n
,
\mathrm{H}^{n}(M|_{Z})
is isomorphic to an iterated extension of elements of
\mathcal{L}(Z)
.

Any such couple can be refined into a couple
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})
such that if
Z
is a stratum in
\mathcal{S}
and
N
belongs to
\mathcal{L}(Z)
, letting
j\colon Z\to X
be the inclusion, the Nori motive
j_{*}(N)
is
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})
-constructible.

This assumption ensures that we can define a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})}(X,\mathbb{Z})
by gluing (in the sense of
[
BBD82
, Section 1.4]
)the t-structure on each stratum
Z
by
-\delta(Z)
. If
(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})
refines
(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
, the t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})}(X,\mathbb{Z})
induces the t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})}(X,\mathbb{Z})
; this is a consequence of the absolute purity property for Nori motives:

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion everywhere of codimension
c
between regular schemes and let
M
be a dualisable object of
\mathrm{DN}(Z,\mathbb{Z})
, then the natural map

i^{*}(M)(-c)[-2c]\to i^{!}(M)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

This follows from the same statement for
\ell
-adic sheaves
[
ILO14
, Exposé XVI, Théorème 3.1.1]
and from the conservativity of the
\ell
-adic realisation
??
and its compatibility with the six functors over quasi-excellent schemes
??
.
∎

To finish the proof of the proposition, note that

\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})}\ \mathrm{DN}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{L})}(X,\mathbb{Z})

and therefore we get a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
by
??
. The t-exactness of the realisation follows from the fact that the perverse t-structure is defined in the same way by gluing along stratifications in
[
BBD82
, Section 2.2]
. The characterisation of the positive and negative parts of the t-structure then follows from the t-exactness of the realisation combined with its conservativity and the analogous characterisation of the positive and negative parts of the perverse t-structure on
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
which is
[
BBD82
, 2.2.12]
.
∎

Remark 5.1.3
.

Along the way we proved that the perverse t-structure on the subcategory
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{lisse}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
made of dualisable objects is the ordinary t-structure shifted by
-\delta(X)
.

This result also implies that any t-exactness property of the perverse t-structure on
\ell
-adic sheaves is true for the perverse t-structure on Nori motives. In particular, the Lefschetz Hyperplane Theorem (also known as Artin Vanishing) is true for Nori motives. The Hard Lefschetz theorem for Nori motives is also true using similar arguments.

Definition 5.1.4
.

Let
X
be an excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension endowed with a dimension function. The
abelian category of perverse Nori motives
is the heart of the perverse t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. We denote it by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

Remark 5.1.5
.

One of the main feats of the perverse t-structure with rational coefficients is that it is preserved by Verdier duality. This is famously not the case with integral coefficients (note for instance that
\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Z})}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[-1]
). However, if
\ell
is a prime number there is a t-structure on
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
that is exchanged with the perverse t-structure by duality (see
[
BBD82
, 4.0 (a)]
).

We can mimic its definition to get a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
as follows; assume that
\mathbb{Z}
is a Dedekind domain and define

{}^{\mathrm{ord}^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0}:=\{M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\mid M\leqslant_{\mathrm{ord}}1\text{ and }\mathrm{H}^{1}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}=0\}

{}^{\mathrm{ord}^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 0}:=\{M\in\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\mid M\geqslant_{\mathrm{ord}}0\text{ and }\mathrm{H}^{0}(M)\text{ is torsion-free}\}

This is a t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
; then by gluing as before this t-structure along stratifications (using the same shifts) we get a t-structure
({}^{p^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0},{}^{p^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 0})
whose heart we denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}^{+}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

Because the six functors are compatible with the
\ell
-adic realisation and because same statement is true after realisation by
[
BBD82
, 3.3.4]
, the Verdier duality functor
\mathbb{D}_{X}
of
??
exchanges the subcategory
{}^{p^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0}
(resp.
{}^{p^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 0}
) with
{}^{p}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\geqslant 0}
(resp.
{}^{p^{+}}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\leqslant 0}
) and therefore it exchanges
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}^{+}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

5.2 Universal motives and the derived category of perverse Nori motives

By the universal property of the bounded derived category, there is a canonical realisation functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z}).

(5.2.A)

This functor is an equivalence after tensorisation by
\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. We have the following rigidity result to compute its torsion:

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Let
X
be an excellent
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes of finite dimension endowed with a dimension function
\delta
.
Let
n
be a prime number.
Consider the composition

\iota_{n}\colon\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\subseteq\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\xrightarrow{f}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})_{tors}\xrightarrow{\iota}\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{tors}

where
\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
is the category of étale perverse sheaves and
f
is the forgetful functor. It lands in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n]
and induces an equivalence

\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n].

Proof.

By
??
the last functor is an equivalence. Moreover as
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})_{tors}
is the kernel of the perverse t-exact functor
-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
, it is endowed with a perverse t-structure, whose heart is the abelian category of torsion perverse motives.

First note that the forgetful functor
f
is t-exact: indeed, the heart of the perverse t-structure on
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})
consists of those complexes
K
such there is a stratification of
X
by nil-regular subschemes such that on each stratum
S
we have
K|_{S}\simeq L[-\delta(X)]
for a lisse sheaf
L
; the image through the forgetful functor of a lisse sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}/n
-modules is a lisse sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, whence the functor
f
is indeed t-exact.

This implies that the functor
\iota_{n}
lands in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(\mathbb{Z})[n]
. Furthermore, the restriction of the forgetful functor to the heart affords a left adjoint, namely
{{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}(-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n)
; it is in fact the underived tensor product with
\mathbb{Z}/n
that sends a perverse sheaf
K
to the cokernel of the map
K\xrightarrow{\times n}K
. With this, it is clear that if
M
belongs to
\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
, we have

\mathrm{H}^{0}(f(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n)=M

and for any object of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})_{tors}^{\heartsuit}
which is of
n
-torsion, we have

f(\mathrm{H}^{0}(N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n))=N.

Whence, the functor
\iota_{n}
indeed induces an equivalence.
∎

Unfortunately, we do not know if the abelian category
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}
has enough torsion-free sheaves. If it was true, we would use
??
to conclude that the functor
??
is fully faithful thanks to
??
and Beilinson’s Theorem on the derived category of perverse sheaves
[
Bei87
]
; this would then imply that this functor is an equivalence. We have a partial answer in that direction, that requires to introduce the universal category of perverse motives. Let
k
be a subfield of the complex numbers (a more general field can be considered, but the following results for those fields can be reduced to subfields of
\mathbb{C}
by the continuity properties of perverse categories considered along fields).

Recall first the theory of universal abelian categories in the form used in
[
IM24
, Section 2.1]
:

Construction 5.2.2
.

Given a triangulated category
\mathrm{T}
, one first consider the category of finite presentation modules over
\mathrm{T}
: it is the full subcategory
\mathrm{fp}(\mathrm{T})\subset\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{add}}(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{Ab})
of additive functors
\mathcal{F}
which fit in a short exact sequence

y_{M}\to y_{N}\to\mathcal{F}\to 0

where
y_{M}
is the image of
M
under the Yoneda embedding. Any cohomological functor on
\mathrm{T}
will factor through
\mathrm{fp}(\mathrm{T})
. Thus, starting with a cohomological functor

\mathrm{H}\colon\mathrm{T}\to\mathcal{A}

with values in an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
,
we have a factorisation of
\mathrm{H}
of the form

\mathrm{T}\to\mathrm{fp}(\mathrm{T})\xrightarrow{\bar{\mathrm{H}}}\mathcal{A}.

Denote by
I
the kernel of
\bar{\mathrm{H}}
. It is the full subcategory of
\mathrm{fp}(\mathrm{T})
consisting of objects
M
such that
\bar{\mathrm{H}}(M)=0
. The universal factorisation of
\mathrm{H}
is the factorisation

\mathrm{T}\to\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{H}):=\mathrm{fp}(\mathrm{T})/I\xrightarrow{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}}\mathcal{A}.

Is has clearly an universal property, and note that composing
\mathrm{H}
with any faithful functor will give the same universal category
\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{H})
. And that any functor comparing with
\mathrm{H}
after composing with an
exact functor
F:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
will also factor uniquely through
\mathcal{M}(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{H})
(because one kernel will be included in the other).
For example, this is the case for
\mathcal{A}=\Lambda\text{-}\mathrm{mod}\xrightarrow{\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda^{\prime}}\Lambda^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}=\mathcal{B}
with
\Lambda^{\prime}
a flat
\Lambda
-algebra.

We apply this to the perverse realisation functor.

Construction 5.2.3
.

Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme. Consider the cohomological functor

{{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Perv}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})

where
\mathrm{Perv}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
is the category of analytic perverse sheaves and
{{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}
is the
0
-th perverse cohomology functor.
As in
??
, we can consider the universal abelian factorisation
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
of this functor through a faithful exact functor
r_{\mathrm{B}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Perv}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
. We thus have a universal homological functor

\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z}).

Because the Betti realisation of perverse Nori motives is faithful exact (as the realisation from
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is conservative), we actually have a finer factorisation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{comp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{N}}}

with
\mathrm{comp}
a faithful exact functor.

Lemma 5.2.4
.

Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme. The functor

\mathrm{comp}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The definition of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
is
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Thus we have to show that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is the universal category for
{{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
. This follows easily from the construction of the universal category together with the fact that the rationalisation of the abelian hull of an additive category if the abelian hull of the rationalisation.
∎

For each nonzero integer
n
, we have an Artin motive functor

\iota\colon\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

such that the composition with the realisation is perverse t-exact and conservative. Thus it induces a faithful exact functor

i\colon\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n].

The following result is an adaptation of a proof due to Nori (
[
Fak00
]
) in the case of fields.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme. Let
n
be an integer. The functor

i\colon\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n]

is an equivalence of quasi-inverse the functor

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{comp}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n]\simeq\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n).

Proof.

We begin by noting three things:

1.

The composite functor
\mathrm{comp}\circ i
lands in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n]=\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
and induces the identity of the latter, because we have that
\mathrm{comp}\circ i={{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}\circ\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\circ\iota
.

2.

The functors
\mathrm{comp}
and
i
are faithful; thus it suffices to prove that
i
is essentially surjective. Indeed, if
i
is essentially surjective, this implies that
\mathrm{comp}
is fully faithful, thus
i
is also fully faithful.

3.

The image of
i
is stable under subquotients. Indeed, if
M
belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})[n]
and is a subobject of something of the form
i(N)
, then
\mathrm{comp}(M)
is a subobject of
\mathrm{comp}\circ i(N)=N
. This implies that
i\circ\mathrm{comp}(M)
and
M
are both subobjects of
i(N)
that are isomorphic after applying the faithful functor
\mathrm{comp}
thus they are isomorphic. For the quotients, one uses that if there is a surjection
i(N)\to M
then the kernel is in the image of
i
thus the cokernel also.

Note also that if
N
belongs to
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, we can write
N/n=\iota(K)
for some
K
in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
, thus

\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N/n)=\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(\iota(K))\overset{(*)}{=}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(\iota({{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}(K)))=i({{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}(K))

is in the image of
i
, where
(*)
follows from the perverse t-exactness of the functor

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z}).

It suffices to show that any object of the form
M/nM
is in the image of
i
, because any
n
-torsion object is of this form. Thus, let
M
be in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and let
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\to M
be a surjective map (this exists by definition of the universal category see
??
). As there is an exact triangle

N\xrightarrow{\times n}N\to N/n

in
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{gm}}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, we have a short exact sequence

\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\xrightarrow{\times n}\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N/n).

Denote by t the image of the map
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N/n)
. Because
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N/n)
is in the image of
i
we see that t is also in the image of
i
as the latter is closed under subquotients. Moreover we have the following commutative diagram with exact rows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M/nM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times n}

where the map
T\to M/nM
exists because the composite of the map
M\to M/nM
with the map
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\to M
vanishes on the kernel of the quotient map
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\to T
. As
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{univ}}(N)\to M
is surjective, this is also the case of the map
T\to M/nM
thus
M/nM
is in the image, finishing the proof.
∎

Thus the functor

\mathrm{comp}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence after being tensored by
\mathbb{Q}
and induces an equivalence on
n
-torsion objects for all
n
. To conclude that it is an equivalence, one could compute some
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, but this seems to be beyond the scope of our current technology. However, the following conjecture seems more reachable:

Conjecture 5.2.6
.

Let
X
be an algebraic variety over
\mathbb{Q}
. Then the abelian category
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
has enough torsion-free object: for every
M
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
there exists an epimorphism
N\twoheadrightarrow M
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
with
N
a torsion-free object.

This conjecture would follow from the equivalence

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{pairs}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z}),

where

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{pairs}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is the universal category associated to a diagram of very good pairs as in Nori’s original construction, as considered by F. Ivorra in
[
Ivo17
]
(he was considering rational motives but his construction remains valid for integral sheaves). This equivalence would follow from the fact that Ivorra’s realisation functor, the main construction in
[
Ivo16
]
is the
{{}^{p}\mathrm{H}}^{0}
of our functor
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}
. Note that in
[
Ivo16
]
the author was only considering smooth varieties but it is not hard to see that a slight modification of his functor (namely, replace the constant sheaf
\mathbb{Q}[-\dim X]
on
X
by
\pi_{X}^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q})}
) would give a functor for any variety. Such a conjecture is related to
[
IM24
, Conjecture 2.12]
, and might be doable with the current technology.

We now explain how
??
implies that both
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
for all
X
but also that
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\simeq\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 5.2.7
.

Assume that
??
holds. Then for every field
k
of characteristic zero and
X
a finite type
k
-scheme, the functor
\mathrm{comp}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is an equivalence, and the functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By continuity along change of fields, it suffices to prove the theorem when
X
is of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}
.
It suffices to prove that the functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

induced by universal property from the functor

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

is fully faithful. Indeed, once it is fully faithful, it will be an equivalence by dévissage because the essential image is then stable under extension. Indeed if
M\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, then there is an short exact sequence

0\to M_{\mathrm{tors}}\to M\to M_{\mathrm{fr}}\to 0

in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
with
M_{\mathrm{tors}}
of torsion and
M_{\mathrm{fr}}
torsion-free. By
??
and
??
the object
M_{\mathrm{tors}}
is in the image. Moreover by
??
there exists
P\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{univ}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
without torsion and a map
P\to M_{\mathrm{fr}}
which becomes an equivalence after tensorisation by
\mathbb{Q}
. In particular, this map has to be injective, and the cokernel is torsion, thus in the image. By fully faithfulness, the object
M_{\mathrm{fr}}
is in the image, whence
M
is in the image.

The functor is an equivalence after tensorisation by
\mathbb{Q}
, and if we tensor the mapping spectra by
\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}
, using
??
(we have enough torsion-free objects by hypothesis!) together with
[
Bei87
]
and rigidity, we conclude. Note that this also proves that
\mathrm{comp}
is an equivalence.
∎

5.3 Applications to Artin motives

Recall that given a scheme
X
and a ring of coefficients
\mathbb{Z}
, the category of Artin motives (resp. constructible Artin motives) is the localising (resp. thick) subcategory of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
generated by the
f_{*}\mathbbm{1}_{Y}
for
f\colon Y\to X
finite (see for instance
[
Rui22
, Definition 2.1.1.1]
); we denote it by
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(X,\mathbb{Z})
(resp.
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
). We likewise define
\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathrm{DN}^{0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme and let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. Then, the Nori realisation functor
\rho_{N}
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(X,\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\Lambda)
. Moreover, the functor
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is t-exact when
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is endowed with the ordinary homotopy t-structure (see
[
Rui22
, Definition 3.1.1.1 and Theorem 3.1.2.7]
).

Proof.

The functors
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(X,\Lambda)
are equivalences. Hence, we can assume that
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}
. Using
??
, it suffices to tackle the case where
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
and the case where
\Lambda=\mathbb{Q}
. The first case follows from rigidity while the second case follows from the main Theorem of
[
Tub26
]
. The t-exactness property follows from the t-exactness of the
\ell
-adic realisations functors on Artin motives (
[
Rui22
, Theorem 3.1.2.7]
) and their conservativity and t-exactness on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.
∎

We will now use Nori motives to give a characterisation of dualisable objects of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. To that end, recall that the category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{sm}0}(X,\mathbb{Z})
(resp.
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{sm}0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
) of smooth Artin motives (resp. constructible smooth Artin motives) is the localising (resp. thick) subcategory of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
generated by the
f_{*}\mathbbm{1}_{Y}
for
f\colon Y\to X
finite
étale
. Using
??
, they are equivalent to full subcategories of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
defined similarly.

Lemma 5.3.2
.

Let
\Lambda
be a commutative ring. For
X
a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme denote by
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\Lambda)
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(X,\Lambda)
made of those objects which are dualisable in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
. The functor
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(-,\Lambda)
is a finitary étale sheaf.

Proof.

As
\mathrm{DM}_{c}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
is finitary by
[
RT25
, Corollary 3.10]
, the functor
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(-,\Lambda)
is also finitary: being Artin and rigid are both finitary conditions. We have to show that
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(-,\Lambda)
is a subsheaf of
\mathrm{DM}_{c}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
, the latter being a sheaf by
[
RT25
, Lemma 2.5]
, meaning that if
f\colon Y\to X
is an étale covering and
M
is an object of
\mathrm{DM}_{c}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
such that
f^{*}M
is dualisable and Artin, then so is
M
. But dualisability is étale local and so is being a
0
-motive.
∎

Proposition 5.3.3
.

Let
X
be a normal
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. The inclusion

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{sm}0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})\subseteq\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence. The t-structure on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
restricts to the full subcategory
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{sm}0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and the heart of the restricted t-structure is equivalent to
\mathrm{Rep}^{A}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X),\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

Using étale descent, continuity and
??
, it suffices to prove the statements if
X
is of finite type over an algebraically closed field.

First note that the inclusion
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DN}^{0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is an equivalence, where the right-hand side consists of the objects of
\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\mathbb{Z})
which are dualisable in
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
. Indeed, if
M
belongs to
\mathrm{DN}^{0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
then its dual is also a
0
-motive, because being a constructible
0
-motive can be tested on points and all constructible
0
-motives over a point are dualisable, with
0
-motivic duals. But now, the t-structure of
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
restricts to
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
because it can be restricted to both dualisable objects and
0
-motives.

By
??
we have a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Q})^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where
\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
is the abelian category of local systems on
X^{\mathrm{an}}
with
\mathbb{Z}
coefficients. By
[
Tub26
]
the category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Q})^{\heartsuit}
is equivalent (through
\rho_{!}
) to
\mathrm{Rep}^{A}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X),\mathbb{Q})
. As the category
\mathrm{Rep}^{A}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X),\mathbb{Z})
also fits in the corner of this pullback diagram, this proves that the functor

\rho_{!}
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Rep}^{A}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X),\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{\mathrm{rig}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\heartsuit}
. Finally, by dévissage, because any object of
\mathrm{Rep}^{A}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X),\mathbb{Z})
defines an object of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{sm}0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, any rigid
0
-motive is smooth Artin.
∎

Using the framework of Nori motives, we can prove new cases of
[
Rui22
, Theorem 3.2.3.7]
which is one of the main results of
loc. cit.
and is an analogue of the Artin Vanishing Theorem in the setting of Artin motives. We begin by the following lemma:

Lemma 5.3.4
.

Let
X
be a qcqs
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme. The ordinary and perverse t-structures on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
restrict to the category
\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
defined as the thick subcategory of
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
generated by the
f_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{Y}
where
f\colon Y\to X
is proper.

Proof.

By
[
Tub26
]
, the result holds for
\mathbb{Q}
coefficients. Moreover it suffices to prove the result over the spectrum of a field
k
. Because the map

\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{c}(k,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{c}(k,\mathbb{Q})

is an equivalence and if
M
is in
\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{c}(k,\mathbb{Z})
then
\mathrm{H}^{i}(M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{i}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is cohomological, there is a cohomological motive
P
in
\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{c}(k,\mathbb{Z})
and a map

P\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(M)
whose cone is torsion and dualisable, thus a constructible
0
-motive, which are cohomological. The claim about the perverse t-structure is true by gluing.
∎

Recall that a smooth Artin motive
M
is
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible if
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
is constructible as a smooth Artin motive. We denote by
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},sm0}_{\mathbb{Q}-c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
the category of
\mathbb{Q}
-constructible smooth Artin motives. We can now prove new cases of
[
Rui22
, Theorem 3.2.3.7]
.

Proposition 5.3.5
.

Let
S
be an excellent scheme
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, let
f\colon X\to S
be an affine quasi-finite morphism. Assume that
X
is regular. Then, the functor

f_{!}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},sm0}_{\mathbb{Q}-c}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(S,\mathbb{Z})

is t-exact with respect to the perverse homotopy t-structure (see
[
Rui22
, Definition 3.2.1.1]
).

Proof.

The same proof as in
[
Rui22
, Theorem 3.2.3.7]
allows to reduce to the case of rational coefficients. We now have to prove that
f_{!}\colon\mathrm{DN}^{sm0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DN}^{0}(S,\mathbb{Q})
is t-exact with respect to the perverse homotopy t-structure (defined in the same fashion as in
[
Rui22
, Definition 3.2.1.1]
).

The rest of the proof is an adaptation of the arguments of
[
Rui24
, Section 4.8]
.
We can write the functor

f_{!}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},sm0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}(S,\mathbb{Q})

as the composition

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},sm0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{DN}^{sm0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}\mathrm{DN}_{c}^{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{f_{!}}\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}_{c}(S,\mathbb{Q})\xrightarrow{\omega^{0}}\mathrm{DN}^{0}_{c}(S,\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},0}_{c}(S,\mathbb{Q}),

where the Artin truncation functor

\omega^{0}\colon\mathrm{DN}^{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\mathbb{Q})

defined as the right adjoint to the inclusion
\iota_{0}
of Artin motives into cohomological motives, lands in
\mathrm{DN}^{0}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Q})
by
[
Tub26
]
.
Thus it suffices to check that all functors are t-exact.
The functor
\omega^{0}
is t-exact with respect to the perverse t-structure on the left-hand side (which exists by the above
??
) and the perverse homotopy t-structure on the right-hand side: this is because an object of
\mathrm{DN}^{0}(X,\mathbb{Q})
is perverse homotopy t-negative if and only if it is perverse t-negative (this can be shown as in
[
Rui24
, Proposition 4.5.1]
) and because the functor
\omega^{0}
is perverse right t-exact as per
[
NV15
, Lemma 3.2.6]
. But as
\iota_{0}
is t-exact when restricted to smooth Artin objects because it is t-exact for the ordinary t-structure, the result reduces to the usual Artin Vanishing Theorem.
∎

5.4 Motivic intersection complex.

Consider an excellent scheme
B
of dimension
2
or less as well as a regular separated
B
-scheme of finite type
S
, endowed with a geometric and
\otimes
-invertible object
\omega_{S}
in
\mathrm{DN}(S,\mathbb{Z})
(note that
\omega_{S}=\mathbb{Z}_{S}
works). We fix
X
a finite type
S
-scheme.

From now on, we assume that
X
is endowed with a bounded below dimension function
\delta
; by shifting, we can assume
0
to be its lower bound.
Any non-increasing function
\overline{q}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{Z}
, defines a perversity function
q=\overline{q}\circ\delta
on
X
along any stratification of
X
by connected regular and locally closed strata (in the sense of
[
BBD82
, Section 2.1.1]
).
The construction given in
??
for
\overline{q}=-\mathrm{Id}
works whenever the condition

(*)\colon\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\ q(n)-q(n+1)\in\{0,1,2\}

is satisfied. It then yields a
q
-perverse t-structure
{}^{q}\mathrm{t}
on
\mathrm{DN}_{\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, whose heart we will denote by
\mathcal{M}_{q\mathrm{-perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and whose cohomology functors are will be denoted by
{}^{q}\mathrm{H}^{n}(-)
.

Definition 5.4.1
.

Let
\overline{q}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{Z}
be a non-increasing function satisfying condition
(*)
, let
q=\overline{q}\circ\delta
and let
j\colon U\to X
be an open immersion.
The
q
-intermediate extension functor
is the functor

{}^{q}j_{!*}\colon\mathcal{M}_{q\mathrm{-perv}}(U,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{M}_{q\mathrm{-perv}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

defined by

{}^{q}j_{!*}M=\mathrm{Im}(^{q}\mathrm{H}^{0}(j_{!}M)\to{}^{q}\mathrm{H}^{0}(j_{*}M)).

The
q
-motivic intersection complex
is the
q
-perverse Nori motive

{}^{q}\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}:={}^{q}j_{!*}(\mathbb{Z}[-q(U)])[q(U)].

When
X
is endowed with a dimension function
\delta
and
q=-\delta
is the self-dual perversity, we simply refer to those as the intermediate extension functor
j_{!*}
and the motivic intersection complex
\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}
.

Remark 5.4.2
.

In
[
Wil12
,
Wil19
]
Jörg Wildeshaus constructed a motivic intersection complex in
\mathrm{DM}(X,\mathbb{Q})
using weights, in several cases (mainy when
X
is a Shimura variety). In those cases, we expect the complex
\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}
to be the Nori realisation of Wildeshaus’ motivic intersection complex up to a shift of
-\dim(X)
. Indeed assuming the existence of the motivic t-structure, this would be true by uniqueness of Wildeshaus’ complex
[
Wil12
, Theorem 3.1]
.

We fix a stratification
(X_{i})
of
X
by regular and connected locally closed subschemes. Given an integer
n
, we set
U_{n}
to be the union of those strata
X_{i}
such that
\delta(X_{i})\geqslant n
and
j_{n}\colon U_{n}\to U_{n-1}
to be the open immersion.

Proposition 5.4.3
.

Let
\overline{q}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{Z}
be a non-increasing function satisfying condition
(*)
and let
q=\overline{q}\circ\delta
. Let
k\geqslant 0
be an integer and let
j\colon U_{k}\to X=U_{0}
be the open immersion. Then there is a canonical isomorphism of functors

{}^{q}j_{!*}\simeq\left(\tau^{\leqslant\overline{q}(0)-1}(j_{1})_{*}\right)\circ\cdots\circ\left(\tau^{\leqslant\overline{q}(k-2)-1}(j_{k-1})_{*}\right)\circ\left(\tau^{\leqslant\overline{q}(k-1)-1}(j_{k})_{*}\right)

on
\mathcal{M}_{q\mathrm{-perv}}(U_{k})
, with
\tau^{\leqslant i}
the truncations functors for the ordinary t-structure.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
[
BBD82
, Proposition 1.4.23]
(see the proof of
[
BBD82
, Proposition 2.1.11]
).
∎

Corollary 5.4.4
.

Let
\overline{q},\overline{r}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{Z}
be non-increasing functions and
q=\overline{q}\circ\delta
and
r=\overline{r}\circ\delta
.
Let
k\geqslant 0
be an integer and let
j\colon U_{k}\to X=U_{0}
be the open immersion.
There exists a canonical pairing in
\mathrm{DN}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})

{}^{q}j_{!*}(M[-q(U_{k})])\otimes{}^{r}j_{!*}(N[-r[U_{k}]])\to{}^{q+r}j_{!*}(M\otimes N[-q(U_{k})-r(U_{k})])

for
M,N\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{\mathrm{rig}}(U_{k})
motivic local systems (that is, objects in the ordinary heart which are dualisable).

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the formula for the intermediate extension given in
??
, knowing that the functors of the form
\gamma_{*}
are lax-monoidal as right adjoints of monoidal functors and that for any
M
and
N
, we have a map

\tau^{\leqslant n}(M)\otimes\tau^{\leqslant m}(N)\to\tau^{\leqslant n+m}(M\otimes N)

given by the right t-exactness of the tensor product and adjunction.
∎

This yields a pairing

\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}^{q}\otimes\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}^{r}\to\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}^{q+r}

in
\mathrm{DN}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.
Now consider the
top perversity

\overline{t}=-2\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Gluing on the stratification as in
[
GM83
, Section 5.1]
, whenever
\overline{q}\leqslant\overline{t}
, there exists a map
\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}^{q}\to\omega_{X}[\delta(X)]
where
\omega_{X}=\mathbb{D}_{X}(\mathbb{Z})
is the dualising complex. Whence a map

\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}\to\omega_{X}[\delta(X)].

(5.4.A)

With rational caoefficients, this pairing induces an equivalence

\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{D}_{X}(\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Q}})[-\delta(X)]

(5.4.B)

by
[
GM83
, Section 5.3]
.
Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero.

Lemma 5.4.5
.

Let
K\in\mathrm{DN}_{c}(k,\mathbb{Z})
and
M\in\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
. For each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
there exists an short exact sequence

0\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{H}^{-i+1}(K),M))\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(K,M))\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{H}^{-i}(K),M))\to 0

in
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

This realises to the classical universal coefficient theorem of abelian groups (or
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-modules if one takes
\ell
-adic realisations).
∎

Definition 5.4.6
.

Assume that
X
is a finite type
k
-scheme. The intersection homology complex of
X
is the object

\mathrm{IH}(X,\mathbb{Z}):=p_{*}\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}

in
\mathrm{DN}_{c}(k,\mathbb{Z})
where
p\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
is the structural morphism. Denote by

\mathrm{IH}_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})=\mathrm{H}^{-i}(\mathrm{IH}(X,\mathbb{Z}))
the
i
-th intersection homology motive. There is also a version with compact support

\mathrm{IH}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z}):=p_{!}\mathrm{IC}_{X,\mathbb{Z}}

and its homology
motive
\mathrm{IH}_{i,c}(X,\mathbb{Z})=\mathrm{H}^{-i}(\mathrm{IH}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z}))

With rational coefficients, we obtain from
??
a perfect pairing

\mathrm{IH}_{i,c}(X,\mathbb{Q})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{IH}_{d-i}{(X,\mathbb{Q})}\to\mathbb{Q}_{k}(-d),

where
d
is the dimension of
X
.

We finish with a motivic lift of the results in
[
GS83
, Section 3]
providing a motivic Seifert linking pairing. The proof of the next proposition is the same as in
loc. cit.

Proposition 5.4.7
.

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
denote by
T_{i}(X)
(
resp.
by
T_{i,c}(X)
) the maximal torsion subobject of
\mathrm{IH}_{i}(X,\mathbb{Z})
(
resp.
of
\mathrm{IH}_{i,c}(X,\mathbb{Z}))
. There is a pairing

T_{i,c}(X)\times T_{d-i-1}(X)\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(-d)

in
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

We apply
??
and obtain a short exact sequence

0\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}_{d-i-1}(X,\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{H}^{-i}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}(X,\mathbb{Z})[d](d),\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}_{d-i}(X,\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z}))\to 0.

Using the exact triangle
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Q}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
and the fact that for any
M\in\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
the object
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,\mathbb{Q}))
vanishes we obtain
that

\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}_{d-i-1}(X,\mathbb{Z})(d),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}_{d-i-1}(X,\mathbb{Z})(d),\mathbb{Z}))

is an equivalence. Moreover because
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
is torsion, the map

\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{T}_{d-i-1}(X)(d),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}_{d-i-1}(X,\mathbb{Z})(d),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))

is also an equivalence (this is true after realisation). Also any map
M\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}_{d-i}(X,\mathbb{Z})(d),\mathbb{Z}))
with
M
torsion vanishes because it can be checked after realisation. Thus the composed map

T_{i,c}(X)\to\mathrm{IH}_{i,c}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{H}^{-i}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}(X,\mathbb{Z})[d](d),\mathbb{Z}))
obtained from applying
\mathrm{H}^{-i}p_{!}
to
??
factors through the inclusion

\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{T}_{d-i-1}(X)(d),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{H}^{-i}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{IH}(X,\mathbb{Z})[d](d),\mathbb{Z})),

yielding

T_{i,c}(X)\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathrm{T}_{d-i-1}(X)(d),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})).

By adjunction (here we use that
\mathcal{M}(k,\mathbb{Z})
has enough flat objects) this provides the desired pairing.
∎

Remark 5.4.8
.

The above pairing is non-degenerate when
X
is smooth. It is not non-degenerate in general, there is a topological obstruction introduced in
[
GS83
]
when
X
is singular and proper.

6 Integral mixed Hodge modules.

6.1 Definition of mixed Hodge modules with integral coefficients

Let
X
be a separated finite type
\mathbb{C}
-scheme. In
[
Sai90
]
, M. Saito constructed an abelian category
\mathrm{MHM}(X)
of mixed Hodge modules over
X
, which is a relative version of mixed Hodge structures, modelled on perverse sheaves, in the sense that if
X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, we have that
\mathrm{MHM}(X)\simeq\mathrm{MHS}^{P}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{C})
is the category of polarisable mixed Hodge structure over
\mathbb{C}
with rational coefficients (which consists of mixed Hodge structure such that the graded pieces of the weight filtration are polarisable
[
Del71
, Définition 2.1.15]
, it is a full subcategory of Deligne’s mixed Hodge structures), and there is a faithful exact functor

\mathrm{rat}\colon\mathrm{MHM}(X)\to\mathrm{Perv}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q}).

Moreover, M. Saito constructed the six operations on the bounded derived category
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}(-))
, in a way compatible with the functor
\mathrm{rat}
.
In
[
Tub25b
]
the second author proved that the construction of these six operations by M. Saito could be canonically lifted to the world of
\infty
-categories, with all possible coherences (this includes an extension to non separated schemes and morphisms). As a consequence, using the universal property of
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})
, the second author obtained a realisation

\rho_{\mathrm{H}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-)

compatible with the six operations, where
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-)
is the indization of
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}(-))
.

In this section, we show that the methods we used for Nori motives apply for mixed Hodge modules, and that proofs are easier in this case.

Definition 6.1.1
.

The presentable
\infty
-category of mixed Hodge modules with integral coefficients is the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}((-)^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{rat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\mathbb{Q}}

where the pullback is taken in the
\infty
-category of functors
(\mathrm{Sch}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{ft}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{\mathrm{St}}
.

Because all categories have a perverse t-structure and that the functors are t-exact, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
affords a perverse t-structure, that restricts to compact objects, over any finite type
\mathbb{C}
-scheme
X
. Moreover,
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
affords a
6
-functor formalism.

Recall that in
[
Del71
, Définition 2.3.1]
Deligne defines mixed Hodge structures that have an integral lattice. In fact, we have a pullback for the abelian categories of polarisable mixed Hodge structures

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MHS}^{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MHS}^{P}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Ab}^{\mathrm{ft}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathrm{fd}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{int}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

with
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathrm{fd}}
the abelian category of finite-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces
(note that the weight filtration only exists with rational coefficients by definition). By definition, we then have that
\mathrm{MHS}^{P}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{C})
is heart of the full subcategory of compact objects in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{C},\mathbb{Z})
.
By the universal property of the fiber product, we obtain a functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{P}(\mathbb{C}))\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H,c}}^{b}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}))

with target bounded objects of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}))
with cohomology in
\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{P}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proposition 6.1.2
.

The above functor is an equivalence.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the cohomological dimension of
\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{P}
is
1
by
[
Bei86
, Corollary 1.10]
.
∎

There is a canonical realisation

\rho_{\mathrm{H}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})

that commutes with the
6
operations. Indeed, this functor is obtained from the universal property of the pullback, and as
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
is compactly generated all operations commute with rationalisation so that the proof consist in proving that the exchange maps are equivalence when rationalised (this follows from
[
CD19
, Theorem 4.2.29]
) and when taken mod
p
for all primes
p
.

6.2 Mixed Hodge modules over
\mathbb{R}
-schemes.

In this section we extend the definition of mixed Hodge modules (with integral coefficients) to
\mathbb{R}
-schemes, using the same method as for Nori motives.

Let
X
be a finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme. Then
X_{\mathbb{C}}:=X\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{R}}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
has a canonical
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
action given by the complex conjugation, and thus the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z})
is endowed with a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
action. In fact this construction is functorial and we obtain a functor

(\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{ft}}_{\mathbb{R}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}))

that sends a finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme
X
to
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z})
.

Now consider the functor

(-)^{\mathrm{h}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}\colon\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}))\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})

that sends a presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
action to the homotopy fixed points
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{h}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}
. Note that this functor take a diagram
G\colon\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
to its limit, thus has a left adjoint
F
that takes an object
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
to the constant diagram
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

Lemma 6.2.1
.

If
X
is a finite type
\mathbb{C}
-scheme, then the natural functor

p_{1}^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z})

induced by the first projection
p_{1}\colon X\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R})}\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})\to X
identifies naturally the left-hand side to the invariants of the right-hand side. More precisely there is a canonical isomorphism between the functor
p_{1}^{*}
and the counit

\eta\colon FG(\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z}).

Proof.

Indeed, one remarks that
X_{\mathbb{C}}\simeq X\times_{\mathbb{R}}X
, and that the projection map
X_{\mathbb{C}}\to X
is an étale cover,
so that the Bousfield-Kan formula for the limit of the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-invariants is exactly the limit for étale descent.
∎

In particular the following definition does not introduce clash of notations:

Definition 6.2.2
.

The functoriality of the presentable
\infty
-category of mixed Hodge modules on finite type
\mathbb{R}
-schemes is the composition

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\colon(\mathrm{Sch}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathrm{ft}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}))\xrightarrow{(-)^{\mathrm{h}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}).

By the previous lemma, the resriction of this functor to finite type
\mathbb{C}
-schemes corresponds to ind-mixed Hodge modules as constructed by M. Saito.
We may denote by
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Q}):=\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proposition 6.2.3
.

Let
X
be a finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme. Then
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
is compactly generated and its full subcategory of compact objects is canonically isomorphic to
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Q}))
where
\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Q})
consists of the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-invariants of
\mathrm{MHM}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})
.

Proof.

Because
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})
has a t-structure that restricts to compact objects, it is true that
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
has a t-structure, that restricts to the invariants
\mathcal{D}(X)
of the compact objects, seen as a full subcategory of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Now if
D
belongs to
\mathcal{D}(X)
, and
M=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}M_{i}
is a filtered colimit of objects of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{map}_{D_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}(D,\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}M_{i})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{map}_{D_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}},\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}(M_{i})_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}})^{\mathrm{h}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{map}_{D_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}},(M_{i})_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}}))

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{R}\Gamma(\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{map}_{D_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}},(M_{i})_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}(D,M_{i})

where
(*)
follows from
[
CD16
, Lemma 1.1.10]
. Thus any object of
\mathcal{D}(X)
is compact. Moreover, because the functor

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})

is conservative, the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(X)
generates
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Q})
under colimits. Thus, the canonical functor
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{D}(X)\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is an equivalence.

Because
p_{1}^{*}
is perverse t-exact, the functor
\mathrm{MHM}(-,\mathbb{Q})
has descent along
p_{1}
. Moreover, because
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}(-,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathcal{D}(-)
is conservative and commutes with
p_{1}^{*}
as well as
(p_{1})_{*}
(which is also perverse t-exact), the same proof as
[
Tub25b
, Theorem 5.23]
gives that
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}(-,\mathbb{Q}))
has descent for the morphism
p_{1}
, but this implies that the functor
\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathcal{D}(X)
is an equivalence, finishing the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.2.4
.

The functor
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
is an étale hypersheaf on finite type
\mathbb{R}
-schemes. For any finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme
X
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
affords a t-structure that restricts to the subcategory
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H,c}}^{b}(X,\mathbb{Z})
of
constructible objects
, which consists of those objects whose underlying ind-constructible sheaf is constructible. We denote by
\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Z})
the heart of the latter
\infty
-category.

Remark 6.2.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth
\mathbb{R}
-scheme.
Let
M\in\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, then
M_{\mid X_{\mathbb{C}}}
admits an underlying
\mathcal{D}
-module on
X_{\mathbb{C}}
with an action of
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, so that in fact we have a functor

\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathcal{D}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}(X)

by Galois descent of
\mathcal{D}
-modules.

Proposition 6.2.6
.

The heart of
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{H,c}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})
is the category
\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(\mathbb{R})
of polarisable mixed Hodge modules over
\mathbb{R}
with integral coefficients. Moreover, the canonical functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}))\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{H,c}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We can identify the heart with the abelian category of polarisable mixed Hodge structures over
\mathbb{C}
with integral coefficients, together with an action of
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, and this is exactly
\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(\mathbb{R})
. Thus we have a functor

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHS}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}))\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{H,c}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z}).

As in the proof of
??
the left-hand side has descent along the morphism
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
, so that the result follows from
??
.
∎

Corollary 6.2.7
.

Let
f\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{R}
be a finite type map, then we recover the absolute Hodge cohomology groups of Beilinson (
[
Bei86
]
) as

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{\mathcal{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z}(p))\simeq\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z})}(\mathbb{Z},f_{*}\mathbb{Z}(p))\simeq\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}(p)).

Proof.

Indeed Saito proved the rational result for
X_{\mathbb{C}}
in
[
Sai90
, Section 1.15]
, thus it suffices to check that Beilinson definition is indeed the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-invariants of a pullback of the rational cohomology and the
\mathbb{Z}
-structure, but this is the case by
[
Bei86
, Section 4.1, Theorem 3.4, Section 7]
.
∎

We finish with a proposition computing the torsion of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 6.2.8
.

Let
X
be a finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme. Then the functor

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})

obtained by taking Galois invariant of the "Artin object functor"

\mathrm{D}((X_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Z})

induces an equivalence

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}.

Proof.

Because everything is obtained by taking Galois invariants, it suffices to prove the claim when
X
is a finite type
\mathbb{C}
-scheme. In that case, the pullback definition provides us with a proof.
∎

6.3 Motivic Hodge modules

As for Nori motives, this realisation functor factors through modules over the algebra
\mathcal{H}
representing Hodge cohomology. Using the same method as
[
Tub25b
, Section 6.2]
, we proved in
[
Tub25a
, Section 2]
that the canonical functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{H}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}}(\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Q}))\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-)

is fully faithful over finite type
\mathbb{C}
-schemes, and that the t-structure on
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-)
induces a t-structure on the image that we describe following Ayoub’s ideas in
[
Ayo22
, Theorem 1.98]
: it is the localising subcategory generated by the
f_{*}\mathbb{Q}(n)
with
f
a proper morphism and
n
an integer, so that we may call those
mixed Hodge modules of geometric origin
(this means that it is the smallest full subcategory stable under all colimits and finite limits that contains the
f_{*}\mathbb{Q}(n)
). This category of modules was first introduced by Drew in
[
Dre18
]
.

There is also an enriched version of this result, where instead of obtaining the localising subcategory generated by the
f_{*}\mathbb{Q}(n)
, we obtain the localising subcategory generated by the
f_{*}H(n)
for any mixed Hodge structure
H
(that we see as a constant mixed Hodge module), that we may call mixed Hodge modules of Hodge origin. Because this theory of enriched modules is more subtle than the geometric origin one, we will explain how to construct an integral version of the enriched category, and leave the case of geometric origin to the careful reader.

Construction 6.3.1
.

The functor
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
naturally takes values in
\mathrm{D}_{H}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})
-linear presentable
\infty
-categories. Thus, the functor
\rho_{\mathrm{H}}
induces a
\mathrm{D}_{H}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})
-linear functor

\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}\colon\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{D}_{H}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z}).

By the good properties of Lurie tensor product, it turns out that the left-hand side is

\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(-,\mathbb{Z}):=\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathrm{D}_{H}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z}))

the presentable
\infty
-category of étale motives with coefficients in mixed Hodge structures which has the same definition as étale motives with coefficient
\Lambda
, except that one replaces
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}
by
\mathrm{D}_{H}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})
. The right adjoint
\bm{\rho^{\mathrm{H}}_{*}}
of
\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}
is lax monoidal thus we get an object
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{X}=\bm{\rho^{\mathrm{H}}_{*}}(\mathbb{Z})
of
\mathrm{CAlg}(\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))
for all finite type
\mathbb{R}
-schemes
X
.
There is a natural map

\pi_{X}^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})}\to\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{X}

with
\pi_{X}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R})
the structural morphism, it will be shown to be an isomorphism.
The presentable
\infty
-categories of integral motivic Hodge modules is the category

\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(X,\mathbb{Z}):=\mathrm{Mod}_{\pi_{X}^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}}(\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(X,\mathbb{Z})).

As for Nori motives, it is also the base change of
\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
from
\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})
-linear categories to
\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})
-linear categories:

\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\simeq\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z})}\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R}),\mathbb{Z}).

Thus as in
??
, the functor

\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
affords all the six operations in such a way that the functor from
\bm{\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
and the canonical functor to
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
commute with them, the composition of the two being
\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}
.

Lemma 6.3.2
.

Let
\iota\colon\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R})
be the spectrum of the inclusion of
\mathbb{R}
into
\mathbb{C}
. Then the canonical map

\iota^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R})}\to\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Becuase
\iota
is finite étale, the functor
\iota^{*}=i^{!}
, being a right adjoint, commutes with
\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}
. Thus
\iota^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R})}\simeq\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}(\iota^{*}\mathbbm{1})\simeq\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})}
.
∎

Theorem 6.3.3
.

The canonical functor

\bm{\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}}\colon\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})

factoring
\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}
is fully faithful and its image is stable under truncations.

Proof.

By étale descent and
??
, it suffices to prove the claim over finite type
\mathbb{C}
-schemes.
Because
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
is compactly generated, the right adjoint
\bm{\rho_{*}^{\mathrm{H}}}
commutes with rationalisation, so that the algebra
\pi_{X}^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})}
, tensored with
\mathbb{Q}
is the algebra considered by Drew in
[
Dre18
]
and the second author in
[
Tub25a
]
. This implies that
\bm{\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}}\otimes\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is fully faithful with image stable under truncations by the main theorem of
[
Tub25a
, Section 2]
(see also
[
Ayo22
, Theorem 1.98]
). When tensoring the square of
??
by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
, we obtain an equivalence

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(-,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Perf}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}),

but the right-hand side coincides with the derived category of étale sheaves of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-modules on
X
, thanks to Artin comparison theorem. By rigidity, this implies that the natural map

\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\to\pi_{X}^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})}/n

is an equivalence, and then that
\bm{\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
is an equivalence. This finishes the proof of the full faithfulness.

Now let
M
be in the image of
\bm{\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}}
. Because the t-structure is compatible with filtered colimits (this is the case for the t-structure of all categories in the pullback defining
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}
), we have an exact triangle

\tau^{\leqslant 0}M\to\tau^{\leqslant 0}(M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\tau^{\leqslant 0}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}.

The middle term is in the image because we proved the theorem with rational coefficients in
[
Tub25a
]
. Thus it suffices to show that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the object
\tau^{\leqslant 0}(M)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is in the image. This is the case because
\bm{\underline{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
is an equivalence. This proves that the image is stable under truncations, finishing the proof.
∎

Remark 6.3.4
.

The fact that
\bm{\underline{\rho}_{\mathrm{H}}}
is fully faithful implies readily that the map
\pi_{X}^{*}\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{R})}\to\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{X}
is an equivalence for all
X
.

Remark 6.3.5
.

As explained at the beginning of this subsection, the above theorem also works for the non enriched version of the Hodge realisation of étale motives. We will call those Hodge modules of geometric origin, and denote them by
\mathrm{DH}(-,\mathbb{Z})
.

Remark 6.3.6
.

For
X
a finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme, the image of
\bm{\rho_{\mathrm{H}}}
can be
described as the localising subcategory
spanned by objects of the form
p_{*}H(n)
for
p\colon Y\to X
proper, and
H
a polarisable integral mixed Hodge structure over
\mathbb{R}
seen as a constant integral mixed Hodge module over
Y
.

6.4 Abelian categories of integral Hodge modules

By definition and by
??
for each finite type
\mathbb{R}
-scheme
X
the three categories
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
,
\mathrm{DH}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\bm{\mathrm{DH}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
admit a perverse t-structure, that restrict to constructible object
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H,c}}^{b}(X,\mathbb{Z})
,
\mathrm{DH}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\bm{\mathrm{DH}}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.
As above, the two categories
\mathrm{DH}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\bm{\mathrm{DH}}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})
are the thick categories spanned by the generators of
??
.

We will denote by
\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Z})
,
\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
the three hearts of the constructible objects.

In more concrete terms, a mixed Hodge module with integral coefficients is the data of three objects
(M,\mathcal{F},S,\varphi)
where
M\in\mathrm{MHM}(X_{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{Q})
is a mixed Hodge module as constructed by M. Saito,
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Perv}(X_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
is a perverse sheaf,
S\in\operatorname{End}(M,\mathcal{F})
is an involution and
\varphi\colon\mathcal{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\simeq\mathrm{rat}(M)
is an isomorphism of perverse sheaves that commutes with
S
.

Because we have the
6
operations, we can also construct ordinary t-structures on the constructible objects by gluing along stratifications, doing the same construction as in the proof of
??
in reverse. We will denote their hearts by
\mathrm{MHM}^{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
,
\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and
\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}^{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
respectively. Using the same proof as
??
, we obtain:

Proposition 6.4.1
.

The natural functors

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}^{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H,c}}^{b}(X,\mathbb{Z}),

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DH}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})

and

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}^{\mathrm{ord}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\bm{\mathrm{DH}}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})

are equivalences.

Remark 6.4.2
.

As for Nori motives, we suspect that the functors

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{DH}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})

and

\mathrm{D}^{b}(\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\bm{\mathrm{DH}}_{c}(X,\mathbb{Z})

are equivalences, because in some sense, objects of geometric origin have a tendency to be flat, or at least, resolved by flat objects.

We finish the paper by linking our notion of mixed Hodge modules to the classical notion of
variation of mixed Hodge structure
. We say that an integral mixed Hodge module
M\in\mathrm{MHM}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is lisse if its underlying perverse sheaf
\mathrm{int}(M)
is locally constant. We denote by
\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
the category of lisse Mixed Hodge modules on a finite-type
\mathbb{C}
-scheme. Note that the pullback defining
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
implies that the perverse t-structure induces a t-structure on the subcategory of dualisable objects of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{H}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
; the heart of this t-structure is precisely
\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 6.4.3
.

Let
X
be a finite-type
\mathbb{C}
-scheme and let
\mathrm{VMHS}_{\mathrm{ad}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
be the category of admissible variations of mixed Hodge structures, where a variation of mixed Hodge structure in the sense of
[
Del80
, 1.8.14]
is admissible if its rationalisation is admissible in the sense of
[
Sai89
, 2.1]
. Then, we have an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{VMHS}_{\mathrm{ad}}(X,\mathbb{Z}).

Proof.

We have a pullback square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q})}

where
\mathrm{Loc}(X,\Lambda)
is the category of locally constant sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules with values a in finitely generated
\Lambda
-modules.

By
[
Sai89
, Theorem 2.2]
, we have an equivalence of categories

\mathrm{MHM}_{\mathrm{lis}}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathrm{VMHS}_{\mathrm{ad}}(X,\mathbb{Q}).

But by definition, a VMHS with integral coefficients is exactly a VMHS with rational coefficients admitting an integral lattice. Hence, we also have a pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{VMHS}_{\mathrm{ad}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{VMHS}_{\mathrm{ad}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Loc}(X^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Q}),}

and the result follows.
∎

Remark 6.4.4
.

One could go further and define the usual operations of mixed Hodge modules, but with integral coefficients. For example if
f\colon X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}
is a map of finite type
\mathbb{C}
-schemes, one can define nearby cycles
\Psi_{f}
associated to
f
as the functor

\Psi_{f}\colon\mathrm{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{H,c}}(X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{H,c}}(X_{s},\mathbb{Z}),

with
X_{\eta}=f^{-1}({\mathbb{G}_{m}})
and
X_{s}=f^{-1}(\{0\})
, defined by the pullback property:

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

so that all known properties about
\Psi_{f}
on
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(-,\mathbb{Z})
remain true for the one for
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{H,c}}(-,\mathbb{Z})
, for example t-exactness or compatibility with duality. Similarly, one can define vanishing cycles, monodromic mixed Hodge modules and have a Thom-Sebastiani formula by pullback. Taking
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-invariants, we also have a version over finite type
\mathbb{R}
-schemes.
